# Changelog

The app shows its build (git commit) in the guide's *Build* badge and at
`GET /api/health`. Updates are fast-forwards of the branch the install is
on — `main` unless it was switched from the Update app card; this file
lists what each batch of changes did, newest first.

## 2026-10-31

### Path coverage: unread conditions never match, Category lookup, UCaaS one-click
- Unread rule conditions never match, for any field. The Path analyzer no longer keeps a list of the conditions it skips: each module (URL filtering, Cloud App Control, SSL inspection, File Type Control, Web DLP, Cloud Firewall, DNS Control, Sandbox, the ATP settings and ZPA access and forwarding rules) lists the fields it evaluates and the fields known not to restrict a match (id, name, order, rank, description, state, last modified, logging, what happens after a match), and any other non-empty field on a rule is an unknown criterion ("Other condition (field)"), so a rule carrying a field a newer API adds is never a definite match: the step is conditional and says why. Every field of the zscaler-sdk-python 1.11.3 rule models is classified (`rule_fields.py`). In the trace such a rule counts as could-apply, never the decision; the what-if never settles it; a ZPA rule with such a field is conditional; an ATP toggle this tool does not know that is on could block, and says so. DNS Control now matches its source IP conditions like the Cloud Firewall.
- **Category lookup.** A third tab on the Path analyzer: type a URL, host name, IP address or category (up to 25) and see its ZIA categories, predefined and custom (ZIA's URL lookup and the tenant's own category lists, with where each came from), the cloud application and any security alert, the custom categories that list it, and every rule across URL filtering, Cloud App Control (by application), SSL inspection, Cloud Firewall (directly or through an IP destination group), DNS Control (requested and resolved categories), File Type Control, Web DLP and Sandbox that names each category, in evaluation order (admin rank first when it is on, the default rule last) with order, rank, action and state. Each Test a list row has a Categories button, and Find anything offers the lookup for any host it is given. Read-only, and only what is in scope on the Scope page; the read is kept for the next lookup (`POST /api/category-lookup/start`).
- **UCaaS one-click** in the ZIA security baseline: Zoom, Microsoft Teams (through Microsoft 365 one-click), Webex, RingCentral, GoTo and Talkdesk, the switches in URL and Cloud App Control advanced settings that make ZIA add its predefined one-click Cloud Firewall, DNS and SSL rules. Picked like Advanced Threat Protection (settings have no disabled state), pre-set on for the Balanced, Permissive and Healthcare tiers and off for Strict, through the same scope check, preview, typed confirmation, re-read before the write, read-back and Undo; the whole settings object is put back with only those switches changed. The Path analyzer reads the switches with the Cloud Firewall and evaluates Zscaler's predefined one-click rules against them: off for every service a rule names, it does not apply.

### Microsoft 365 app service groups matched by domain and address
- **Microsoft 365 app service groups are matched.** ZIA's predefined Office 365 One Click rule names the app service group OFFICE365, and the ZIA API returns only its name, never its members, so the Path analyzer (Cloud Firewall, DNS Control and workload forwarding rules), its what-if and Test a list always called it "unknown". With Microsoft's published Microsoft 365 endpoint list they now say match, and why ("outlook.office365.com is in Microsoft 365 'Exchange Online' (endpoint set 1: TCP 80, 443; UDP 443; list version 2026100100, fetched 2026-10-31)"), no match (an address in none of the published IPv4 or IPv6 ranges) or, as before, unknown: a host name the list does not name (its address is not resolved), a port Microsoft does not publish for the set, an app service that is not Microsoft 365, no list, or a list not confirmed for 30 days. Host names follow Microsoft's wildcards (`*.sharepoint.com` is any name below it, not sharepoint.com; `<tenant>` and any other `*` stand for one label part), in lower case and punycode. Shadowed rules now reports a later Cloud Firewall rule whose destinations are all Microsoft 365 endpoints, on ports Microsoft publishes for them, as shadowed by the One Click rule above it, saying which list it used.
- **Microsoft 365 endpoint list** (Advanced settings). Off by default. **Fetch Microsoft's list** turned on, the app fetches it from `endpoints.office.com` (Microsoft's Office 365 IP Address and URL web service, worldwide instance): the version first, the list only when it changed, at most once a day when you analyze, or at once with **Fetch now**; a random request id, nothing about the tenant. On an offline machine, **Import the list from a file** instead (saved from the same address elsewhere, with its version if you know it). Either way it is checked as untrusted (2 MB at most, every set, host name, address and port) and refused whole when it is not Microsoft's list, then kept owner-only with its version and date; **Remove the list** forgets it. The sample tenant has the One Click rule and a short excerpt of the list to show it. `endpoints.office.com` is listed with the app's other outbound destinations in SECURITY.md.
- **Test a list** and **Shadowed rules** use the same list: a row on a Microsoft 365 address or host name is decided by the One Click rule instead of being conditional on it, and a later firewall rule it hides is reported with the list named.

### A safe rule editor, and the rest of the create, edit and delete
- **Edit a rule.** A new Bulk changes form (Build new) creates, edits, duplicates or deletes one URL filtering, Cloud Firewall, SSL inspection, forwarding control, Inline Web DLP, DNS Control, IPS, File Type Control, Sandbox or Cloud App Control rule (*Create a rule*, *Edit a rule*, *Duplicate a rule*, *Delete a rule*). Each rule type has its own form with only the fields its zscaler-sdk-python 1.11.3 model has (a test builds every model from a full payload), checks every value (enumerations from the SDK, IP addresses, ranges, CIDR blocks and domains, country codes, ids, list sizes, names within ZIA's 31 characters, no control characters), and picks categories, groups, departments, users, locations, labels, time intervals, network services, IP groups, applications, gateways and DLP engines by name (each engine says which dictionaries it uses). The preview says in one sentence what the rule will do ("This rule will block the URL categories Gambling for the groups Finance, placed at order 2, rank 5"), lists every field before and after, and explains the placement: with Admin Ranking on, a new rule takes the rank of the rule it sits above (going last, of the rule just above it), otherwise rank 7. New rules and copies are created disabled unless *Create it enabled* is ticked; a duplicate sits right below its source at its rank. An edit sends the rule back as read with only the changed fields, so nothing the form does not show is lost. A change that is live at once needs the typed phrase; a delete needs DELETE and the rule's name. Every write goes through the Bulk changes engine: the Scope page, the four gates, the plan hash, a re-read before each write (a rule someone changed meanwhile is left alone), read-back, the audit log with the actor, and Undo (it deletes what was created, puts back each changed field, or re-creates a deleted rule with the same fields, order, rank and state).
- **Enable or disable DNS and IPS rules.** Enable or disable rules now covers DNS Control and IPS (Cloud Firewall) rules too, in scope when ZIA platform is ticked.
- **Edit a ZPA application segment.** Change one segment's name, description, bypass, health reporting, ICMP, double encryption, connector choice, keep-alive and ports, or enable or disable it; the segment's domains, servers and group are sent back as read. ZPA applies at once, so any change to an enabled segment needs the typed phrase.
- **Cloud app instances and tenancy restrictions.** Browse the cloud application instances and tenancy restriction profiles (read-only), and create or edit one through the SDK; Undo deletes what was created.
- **Proxy chaining.** Create or edit a third-party proxy and see which proxy gateways use each one. The SDK can list gateways but not create them, so the preview and the result list the steps to do in the ZIA Admin Portal (the gateway, its root certificate, activation, then the forwarding rule, which Edit a rule can create).

### Backups, restore, clone and templates for ZIA, ZPA and Client Connector
- **Configuration backups.** Bulk changes → Backups saves a read-only backup of ZIA, ZPA or Client Connector: every object type the Zscaler SDK 1.11.3 can read (85 types; `config_backup.coverage()` and the page list which are restored, updated only or kept for comparison, and why), only what is in scope on the Scope page, kept beside the tenant profile as owner-only files with every secret masked. A saved snapshot or the sample tenant can be kept as a backup too (offline, no tenant read).
- **Compare field by field.** Two backups, a backup and the live tenant, or a backup and the configuration cache: added, removed and changed objects with each changed field's old and new value; ids, change times, who changed it and counters are ignored (and any field you add); references compare by name, so a clone's new ids are no difference.
- **Restore and clone.** Three operation types on the Bulk changes engine (ZIA, ZPA, Client Connector): add what is missing, update what differs, or match exactly with deletes (typed DELETE n OBJECTS). The plan is in dependency order, starts with a fresh automatic backup of the target, re-reads each object before its write, stops at the first failure or conflict, reads back, activates ZIA as its own shown last step, and records an exact Undo. A clone remaps every reference by name, lists what cannot be cloned and why, never copies a secret (a VPN credential needs its pre-shared key typed, held in memory for the apply only), compares the two tenants' subscriptions first (a gap blocks until acknowledged), writes only to the tenant of the saved profile you have open, and names the manual steps (locations, static IPs, GRE tunnels, the proxy gateway). Settings can be restored key by key; proxies and their root certificates are cloned for proxy chaining. Opt-in rollback undoes a stopped or failed run at once; a re-push plan for what did not arrive is offered after the read-back.
- **Rehearsal.** With the sample tenant or a saved snapshot open, a restore previews but is never applied; Rehearse (plan only) plans against the sample tenant or a saved backup without building any client.
- **Licence check.** Before a clone, the source backup's ZIA subscriptions are compared with the target's; one the target lacks blocks the plan until you tick that you checked the licences.
- **Templates.** Pick objects in a backup (what they use comes with them): ids removed, names start with a prefix you choose when you apply it to any tenant through the same plan; import and download as a file (checked as untrusted).
- **Scheduled backups.** Per saved profile, every 1 to 168 hours, keeping the newest 1 to 100: read-only, only the profile's own scope, only when the profile remembers its secret; nothing is ever applied on its own.
- **Configuration cache.** A per-profile cache of every object with a hash, refreshed on demand or by the schedule, saying what was added, removed or changed since the last refresh, and used for compares that read no tenant.

### ZPA identity in the Path analyzer and the ZPA builder
- **ZPA identity in the Path analyzer.** The ZPA step no longer treats every user as "any user". In What-if, pick the IdP the user signs in with (Load IdPs from ZPA), type their SCIM user name to look their SCIM groups up (read-only, kept only for that analysis), and type SAML or SCIM attribute values as Name=value. ZPA's access policy is then evaluated for that identity with ZPA's rules: a condition's operands joined by its operator (OR unless it says AND), a negated condition inverted, the conditions joined by the rule's operator, the first matching enabled rule in rule order, default deny. A SCIM group, SCIM attribute or SAML attribute of another IdP is not met, nor one whose IdP has SCIM-based or SAML-based policy off. Anything not given or not read stays "unknown" with the reason, never a match. The verdict says which identity condition decided ("allowed by “Finance apps” because SCIM group “Finance” matched"), which earlier rules do not apply to this user and why, and what is still unknown. A lookup needs ZPA ticked on the Scope page (refused before sign-in otherwise); a filter ZPA ignored is detected and the groups stay unknown; logs carry counts, never a user name, group or value.
- **ZPA identity column.** Test a list takes idp, SCIM user and attributes columns: each distinct user is looked up once per run (at most 200), and each row's ZPA answer says which identity condition decided.
- **Access rules for SCIM groups and SAML attributes.** The ZPA builder's access rule can allow only users in SCIM groups or with SAML attribute values picked from what the tenant has (IdPs, SAML attributes and SCIM groups read for the form), each checked again in the preview, through the same four write gates, read-back and Undo. With both kinds picked a user needs one of each (the SDK puts each kind in its own condition).

### Static analysis (SAST) in every test run and in CI
- **bandit, offline, in every test run.** The Python static analyser bandit (1.9.4, hash-pinned in `requirements-dev.lock` with its dependencies) checks the app's Python with every check on; `tests/test_sast.py` fails on any finding of medium severity or higher, so CI fails a pull request that adds one. Run it locally with `make sast` (after `make dev`). The configuration is `pyproject.toml` `[tool.bandit]`: no skipped checks, only `tests/` and local environments left out.
- **CodeQL in CI.** `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` runs GitHub's security-extended queries over the Python and the JavaScript (web UI and browser extension) on pull requests to `dev`, `qa` and `main` that change code, and weekly: one Linux job, read-only token except `security-events: write` on that job, actions pinned by commit SHA, stale runs cancelled, 20-minute limit. It has not run yet (Actions minutes are used up); on a private repository code scanning needs GitHub Advanced Security.
- **Every current finding triaged.** One fixed: two id hashes (guidance links, pack ids from non-Latin names) now say they are not a security use (`usedforsecurity=False`), so they also work on FIPS-mode OpenSSL; the ids are unchanged. Eleven false positives (an address compared, never bound; XML parsed only after DOCTYPE/ENTITY are refused; the standalone smoke test's own loopback requests and zip extraction; an update message that looked like SQL) are explained on their line, seven reviewed suppressions in all, and the test fails on any unlisted, unexplained or stale one. The 232 low-severity notes were read and recorded by kind in `docs/qa/findings/w7-sast.json`. This closes the QA check "no SAST in CI".

### Day-to-day admin for ZPA, Client Connector and ZDX
- **ZPA day-to-day admin.** On Bulk changes, App Connectors, Private Service Edges and their groups are listed with their health (connected, disconnected or turned off, current and expected version, last connection) and can be renamed, turned on or off, an App Connector moved to another group, and a group's software update day, hour and version profile set; PRA portals, PRA consoles and user portals can be turned on or off. Undo puts every changed field back. Delete covers App Connectors, Service Edges, their groups, PRA portals and consoles, user portals and Browser Access certificates, and revokes provisioning keys: the typed confirmation always names what goes (`DELETE <NAME>`, or the count), and anything still connected or used is kept and named; there is no Undo, and the preview says so.
- **ZPA provisioning keys.** A new key for an App Connector group or a Service Edge group is created off unless turned on, shown once and never kept; Undo revokes it.
- **ZPA Browser Access certificates.** A certificate is uploaded with its private key (checked against the certificate, held in memory ten minutes for the person who uploaded it, sent to ZPA once, never logged, stored or shown) and applications are pointed at it, or at one ZPA has; each certificate shows its expiry, an application the certificate does not cover is left out, and Undo points the applications back and deletes the upload.
- **Client Connector day-to-day admin.** Remove (Removal Pending) or force remove a user's devices with a typed `REMOVE <n> DEVICES` every time; list, create (off by default), edit and delete custom IP-based bypass apps, never deleting one an app profile bypasses; set the ZPA and ZDX entitlements (see below).
- **Custom bypass apps and entitlements.** Custom IP-based bypass apps are created off by default and never deleted while an app profile bypasses them; the ZPA and ZDX entitlements are on for every user or for the groups listed, with the typed confirmation when access narrows. Undo for bypass app creates and edits and for entitlements.
- **Remove devices from a list.** Paste or upload users, host names, device ids, user and host name pairs or @domains; they are matched against one read of the enrolled devices (device id first, then pair, user, host name, domain), with ambiguous host names and unmatched entries shown to settle before the preview, an optional domain filter and counts per domain. Up to 2000 devices by explicit device id, in batches of a chosen size, after a check against Client Connector's 100 API calls an hour; a verification read afterwards; the plan and the result download as CSV (spreadsheet-safe, with a counts-only option, since names are personal data).
- **ZDX deep trace and look-ups.** Start a deep trace on a user's device with the API's options (application, Web and CloudPath probe, 5 to 60 minutes, device health), follow it a bounded number of reads and see its results, and stop it (typed confirmation, no Undo); starts are rate-limited per tenant and per device, and Undo of a start stops it. Read-only look-ups: application scores and their trend, users by name or email, a device's details and health, and an application's performance on a device. Every read and change needs its product ticked on the Scope page.

### New rules take the admin rank they need
- **Rank inherited, not fixed at 7.** With Admin Ranking on (ZIA advanced settings, `enableAdminRankAccess`, read only when ZIA platform is ticked), a rule the app creates at order N takes the admin rank of the rule now at order N, the rule it is placed above, or, going last, of the rule just above it: AI Guard routing (the QUIC block, the SSL Inspect rule at the top, the forwarding rule, the Cloud App Control and URL filtering allows, and its own rules moved back to the top), the ZIA security baseline (rules added below the tenant's, the SSL catch-all kept last), a policy pack deploy (Web, Endpoint and containment rules at order 1, SaaS Security API rules last of their type; *Use rank 0* still wins) and a Bulk changes reorder (a moved rule keeps its rank when it fits between its new neighbours, otherwise takes the rank of the rule it now sits above). Off or unknown, rules get rank 7 as before, and the preview says when the setting was not read. An SSL certificate move keeps each rule's rank and order.
- **Never a rank the API client cannot assign.** The Zscaler SDK has no call for the API client's own admin rank, so it is inferred from the rules ZIA lets it edit (marked assumed). When the rank a rule needs is above it, the rule gets a rank the client may assign and the preview warns that it lands lower (for example AI Guard's order-1 SSL Inspect rule below rank 0-6 rules, so it is not first); when nothing shows the client's rank, the preview says ZIA may refuse it.
- **Preview.** The Bulk changes preview lists each new or moved rule's rank, order and why in *Admin rank of the rules this change places*; Deploy policy packs shows it on each new rule. The typed confirmation still applies.
- **Read-back.** It compares the rank ZIA stored with the one sent (and the order, where nothing else in the run moves it) and names both when they differ; such a run is reported as partly applied, and a pack's verification fails.
- **Undo.** The Undo of a reorder puts each changed rank back with the order; the AI Guard and baseline Undo put back the rank they changed (a rule they created is deleted).

### Federal compliance profiles and the compliance pack
- **Compliance profile.** One choice, made once in the ZIA security baseline builder (Bulk changes, Build new) and remembered for the saved tenant profile, drives the baseline's tier and overlay, the rules the profile adds or requires, the DLP policy packs offered, the AI Guard detection profile and the compliance pack; a box says what it will do before anything is built. Six federal profiles, each with its own control catalogue and cited source: FedRAMP Moderate and FedRAMP High (NIST SP 800-53 Rev. 5 controls of the SP 800-53B baselines), CMMC Level 2 (the NIST SP 800-171 Rev. 2 requirements as CMMC practices), Federal Zero Trust (OMB M-22-09 and CISA ZTMM v2.0), IRS Publication 1075 (federal tax information, with 26 U.S.C. 6103(p)(4)) and GovRAMP Moderate. All build on Strict, the only tier with the firewall default deny that SC-7(5) and 3.13.6 ask for, and change rules only where the source justifies it: FedRAMP High requires the SSL catch-all, the QUIC block and the password-protected archive block and leaves out the privacy exemptions (AC-4(4)); CMMC Level 2 and IRS 1075 add a block of public translation services (CUI and FTI flow); IRS 1075 requires the personal webmail block; Federal Zero Trust requires DNS over HTTPS inspection and the Trusted Resolver (M-22-09's encrypted DNS). GovRAMP reuses FedRAMP Moderate and says so. The nine existing overlays are framework profiles in the same list; saved builds without a profile work as before.
- **Every rule mapped.** Every rule the baseline or an offered policy pack creates, and every Advanced Threat Protection setting, maps rule -> objective -> the profile's controls, or carries a reason code (OPERATIONAL, NOT_IN_SCOPE_FOR_PROFILE, VENDOR_DEFAULT, PRIVACY_EXCEPTION, CAUTION_ONLY, ALLOWS_TRAFFIC, NOT_CREATED); a rule with neither fails the build and the tests. Controls not verified against their source are marked assumed everywhere they show.
- **Compliance pack.** Local files only, never a tenant write, through the app's authenticated routes: a control mapping report (HTML, CSV and Excel), a policy export of what would be built (or, from a recorded run, what was), a test plan by control, and one synthetic DLP test file per DLP rule. Every test file starts with SYNTHETIC TEST DATA, uses reserved example SSNs, published test card numbers and the markings themselves, and says which dictionaries it covers and whether the engine should fire (evaluated locally).
- **IRS 1075 FTI Pack.** A new policy pack: an FTI markings dictionary (the IRC 6103 citation, the words Federal Tax Information) and return data (tax form and transcript names near an SSN or TIN), monitor-only and disabled on Web, Endpoint, SaaS and Email DLP, with its Terraform workspace.
- **AI Guard federal profiles.** NIST SP 800-53, NIST SP 800-171 / CMMC and IRS Publication 1075 detection profiles beside the existing eight; the AI Guard detection policy form follows the compliance profile.
- **Read against a profile.** The Regulatory & compliance alignment page reads an existing tenant's assessment against any profile: each control met, partially met, not met or not tested, with the checks behind it, and a CSV.
- **Federal and public sector preset.** The POV planner has a Federal and public sector vertical and a compliance profile in its intake (the Test kit tab offers the profile's compliance pack and a federal DLP test file); Simple mode's Protect task asks which compliance rules apply before it offers rule sets.

### ZIA governance and audit
- **ZIA governance and audit checks.** Eight new ZIA platform checks in section `ZIA — Governance`, run when ZIA platform is ticked on the Scope page. Read-only, only what is ticked; every new read (`/configAudit`, `/alertSubscriptions`, `/nssServers`, `/nssCollectors`, `/subscriptions`, `/passwordExpiry/settings`, `/remoteAssistance`, as zscaler-sdk-python 1.11.3 reads them) is answered by the sample tenant and kept in snapshots.
  - **Zscaler's configuration audit**: the System Audit Report mapped into findings, its overall grade and each graded item (high availability and GRE tunnels, authentication frequency, PAC files, Microsoft 365, client IP visibility and user performance) with Zscaler's recommended configuration. Refused for lack of the Reports scope, it is "Not permitted", never judged; no report yet is informational.
  - **Alert subscriptions**: every alert class (Patient 0, Secure, Manage, Comply, System) reaches someone, and more than one recipient; recipients are counted, never listed.
  - **NSS server health**: every NSS VM enabled and healthy, beside the feeds.
  - **Log streaming out of ZIA**: a Cloud NSS feed, a healthy NSS server or an NSS collector carries the logs to a SIEM.
  - **N/A when not licensed**: the NSS checks are N/A (outside the score) when the organisation's subscriptions are read and hold no NSS SKU; an unreadable subscription list never marks anything N/A.
- Two new RBAC checks.
  - **Admin password expiry**: expiration off, or longer than 180 days, needs review (admins on SSO follow the IdP).
  - **Zscaler Support access**: full access to the ZIA admin portal, or view-only access open for more than 30 days, needs review; user names not obfuscated for Support are called out.
- The snapshot scrubber now keeps the admin password expiry switch and its days (a true/false and a number, no secret): before, they were masked like a password, and a snapshot of a tenant with expiry off could pass. 59 new tests in tests/test_w8_zia_governance.py.

### ZPA admin and RBAC audit
- **ZPA admin and RBAC audit.** Eleven new read-only RBAC checks (RBAC.13 to RBAC.23) cover ZPA administration. Each says why it matters and where to fix it in the ZPA Admin Portal; a feature the tenant does not have (microtenants, PRA) is N/A, and a refused read is not judged.
  - **ZPA admins and second factor**: enough enabled admins but not too many, and a second factor for every admin who can sign in with a local password.
  - **SSO-only admin sign-in**: admin login follows the IdP's MFA and offboarding.
  - **Full-access and read-only roles**: few admins with a full-access role, and a read-only role and task-scoped roles.
  - **ZPA API keys**: least privilege and tokens valid for a day at most.
  - **Break-glass accounts**: an emergency access account exists and stays dormant.
  - **Microtenant admins**: an admin of its own in each microtenant (read per microtenant, at most 20).
  - **Provisioning keys**: capped near the enrolments they need and not left unused.
  - **Privileged Remote Access credentials and approvals**: credentials rotated within 90 days and pooled, and no approval open for more than 30 days.
- Read only with RBAC and ZPA both ticked on the Scope page (the RBAC entry lists every new endpoint). Every list is scrubbed as it is read: a password, client secret, token or provisioning-key value never reaches a check, a result, a log or a snapshot, and admins are counted, never listed. Snapshots save the new lists; the sample tenant has made-up data for each, with gaps to find.
- Paths verified against the installed Zscaler SDK 1.11.3: PRA credential pools are served by their own ZPA service base (waap-pra-config), now resolved and normalised; admin SSO login options sit under /v2. A snapshot used to mask a ZPA API key's token lifetime as if it were a token; a numeric lifetime is now kept.

### Rules from a spreadsheet and scheduled sync
- **Rules from a spreadsheet** (Bulk changes, two new types: ZIA and ZPA access policy). *Read from the tenant*, then *Download the rules as CSV* for Cloud Firewall, URL filtering or the ZPA access policy: one row per rule, top first, with documented columns, names instead of ids (locations, groups, network services, custom URL categories, application segments, SCIM groups as `IdP::group`), the order and the admin rank; every cell is safe to open in a spreadsheet (a leading `= + - @` TAB or line break gets a quote, and reading the file back gives exactly the value). Edit it offline and choose it: choosing only checks the file (schema, values, duplicates, 1000 kB / 2000 rules / cell caps; `POST /api/rule-csv/validate`, no tenant read). *Preview* resolves every name in the tenant and plans adds, updates (only the columns the file has), moves (the fewest single-rule moves for ZIA, one bulk reorder for ZPA) and deletes, which happen only when you tick *Remove rules not in the file* and type `DELETE <n> RULES`. A row naming something the tenant does not have, a locked rule (Default, predefined, read-only) or a ZPA condition the file cannot express is excluded with the reason. New ZIA rules are created disabled; new ZPA access rules (which cannot be created disabled) only with *Create new rules enabled*. Applying goes through the usual gates, re-reads every rule before its write, activates ZIA once, reads back and records an Undo that deletes what was added, puts back changed fields, re-creates deleted rules and restores the order. Scripts: `GET /api/rule-csv/format`, `POST /api/rule-csv/export`.
- **Scheduled sync** (Change queue page). A sync job keeps rules of up to ten target tenant profiles in step with a source (a tenant profile, a saved snapshot or a saved configuration template): ZIA Cloud Firewall, URL filtering and/or the ZPA access policy, optionally only rules with a rule label or a name prefix, by hand or every 1, 4, 12, 24 or 168 hours. Each run only reads: per target and policy it plans the change and files it in the change queue as a pending *Bulk change plan* with its plan hash, in the job owner's name (or whoever clicked *Run now*), so another writer approves it. A newer plan marks the older open one stale; an unchanged one is not filed twice; a target whose saved scope lacks the policy is not read. Deletes are planned only when the job asks, within its selection, and need `DELETE <n> RULES` when applied. Applying an approved plan needs write mode, the assessor, the typed confirmation and the target's credentials, and plans the target again: a target that changed since marks the request stale and nothing is written. Each job keeps its run history; a run that filed plans is announced through the digest's e-mail / webhook settings when set. The server's timer (`DLP_CHECK_SYNC_TIMER`, on; each job's own schedule decides) starts due jobs one at a time. Templates can be a source once a template provider registers (`sync_jobs.register_template_source`).

### Wave 7: closing the gaps a comparison with other Zscaler tools found
- **ZIA users, groups and departments.** A new *Day-to-day admin* group on Bulk changes (needs ZIA platform ticked on the Scope page). Find users by name (ZIA's own "starts with" search; a large directory is searched, never read whole), add a user with a department and groups, change users' department and groups, add, rename or delete groups and departments, up to 100 changes in one plan. The usual engine: the Scope page checked before any read, a preview with a plan hash, write mode, a writer, the assessor's name and the confirmation, a re-read of every object right before its write (anything changed meanwhile is left as it is), one activation, read-back, the audit log with the actor, and Undo (a created object is deleted, an edit is put back). A delete always asks its name typed (*DELETE <name>*) and the preview says it cannot be undone. Admin accounts, predefined groups and groups or departments that come from the identity provider are left alone, with the reason; no password is ever set or read. Disabling a ZIA user is not offered: the Zscaler SDK's user model has no such field (the page says so).
- **ZIA PAC files.** On the same page: every hosted PAC file with its versions and its active text (shown as text, never run). Validate a PAC (this app's own parser, which never executes it, and ZIA's validator), add a new version (created staged, so nothing uses it until it is deployed), create or clone a PAC file, switch the active version, or delete a file (its name typed). The preview shows a line diff against the version it replaces or comes from, and both checks; ZIA's validator refusing the text blocks the plan. Undo deletes a created or cloned file and switches back to the version that was active; a new version and a delete cannot be undone (the SDK has no call that deletes one version), and the preview says so.
- **ZIdentity users and API clients.** With ZIdentity ticked on the Scope page (and a OneAPI sign-in): find users by login name, add users (disabled unless you tick enabled; no password is set), disable, enable or delete them (a delete asks its name typed and cannot be undone); administrators and users from SCIM are left alone. List the API clients with their scopes (a full-access scope is flagged), create a client with only the scopes you tick (none is ticked for you; created disabled unless you tick enabled) and its first secret, rotate a client's secret (*ROTATE <name>*; the old secrets are deleted only once the new one exists, or kept if you ask), disable, enable or delete a client (*DELETE <name>*). A new secret is shown once, to the person who ran the apply, from an in-memory store that forgets it after one view or ten minutes; it is never in a plan, result, log, audit entry, change record or export. The API client this app signs in with is never changed here. Resetting a user's password or MFA is not offered: Zscaler SDK 1.11.3 has no call for either (the page says so).

### Ask an AI assistant about the tenant: read-only analysis tools and a skill
- **The MCP server answers live questions with the app's engines.** Besides the assessment, `dlp_mcp_server.py` now has fifteen read-only tools (`mcp_tools.py`): `list_sources`, `path_check` (the Path analyzer for one destination, with what-if user, group, department, location, network and platform), `test_list` (up to 50 destinations), `rule_precedence` (rule order and shadowed rules), `explain_dlp` (a DLP engine or rule in plain words, a channel's rules, or an expression), `data_flow` (the Data Flow Map), `ssl_coverage`, `dlp_incidents` (the Live DLP feed counted by rule and enforcement; users counted, not named), `traffic_summary` (Traffic insights, no per-user rows), `zpa_path`, `fleet_health` (counts only), `snapshot_diff` (against the live tenant or another snapshot), `category_lookup` (up to 25 URLs), `readiness` and `compliance_map` (the last assessment's controls). Each works on the live tenant, the sample tenant or a saved snapshot.
- **Read-only, twice over; no write tool at all.** Every client a tool builds refuses PUT, PATCH, DELETE and every POST but the URL lookup, and every HTTP request the server sends passes a guard that lets through only reads (GET, the sign-in token, the URL lookup, the Insights query, the incident search); the server process turns write mode off. Changes stay in the app's pages with their gates. The assessment tools run inside the same guard.
- **Same sign-in and the Scope rule.** Credentials come from the server's environment only, as before. What is in scope is chosen where the server runs (`ZSCALER_MCP_PROFILE`, a saved profile's Scope page ticks, or `ZSCALER_MCP_SCOPES` and `ZSCALER_MCP_CHANNELS`), never by the assistant; with nothing chosen every live read is refused before sign-in. Answers are summaries of at most about 48,000 characters, masked for every credential and secret field, with tenant text marked as untrusted data (a rule name can carry instructions); one tenant read runs at a time.
- **A skill for assistants.** `skills/zscaler-made-easy/` routes an admin's questions to the tools (a routing table), explains how to read each answer, gives plain answer templates, the data security flows and their walkbacks ("why didn't DLP catch it"), and SIEM query templates (Splunk and Sentinel, field names to check against the feed) to prove what happened. `docs/AI_ASSISTANT.md` says how to connect the server and the skill to Claude Code, Claude Desktop or another assistant.
- Tests: tests/test_mcp_tools.py (schemas and caps of every tool, read-only guard and sealed clients, scope refusals before sign-in, secret canaries, people counted not named, the routing table names only real tools).

### Firewall migration from Palo Alto Networks, a terminal UI, local plugins and update notifications
- **Firewall and private access.** Migrate from another vendor now reads a Palo Alto Networks PAN-OS configuration export (firewall or Panorama, XML) and builds a migration plan: security rules become ZIA Cloud Firewall rules (allow, deny, drop and reset mapped to ALLOW, BLOCK_DROP and BLOCK_RESET), address objects and groups become IP source and destination groups, services and service groups become network services, applications become network applications where one exists, and allow rules from a GlobalProtect zone to internal addresses become ZPA application segments and an access rule. Zones are noted; everything that cannot be carried exactly (negated matches, users, URL categories, schedules, HIP profiles, web and SaaS applications, wildcard and dynamic addresses, IPv6, SCTP, NAT, decryption, GlobalProtect portals and gateways, a second catch-all) is listed with the reason and never trimmed. The plan downloads as JSON, and its ZPA part as the CSV the ZPA builder reads (**Download the ZPA segments CSV**). The export is parsed with defusedxml (now in the hash-pinned lock: no DTD, entities or external references), capped in size, depth and element count.
- **Firewall import from Palo Alto Networks.** A new Build new operation on Bulk changes creates the plan's groups, services and rules: previewed against the tenant (an object with the same name and other content is never changed, and blocks the rules that need it; a network application the tenant lacks excludes the rule), applied through the four gates with the typed phrase, every rule DISABLED at the bottom of the policy, read back, and undone by deleting what it created. **Open in Bulk changes** on the migration page hands the plan over in the same tab. The ZPA part goes through the existing ZPA builders with the downloaded segments CSV.
- A terminal UI: `zscaler-made-easy tui` (or `dlp-check tui`) opens a keyboard-only, curses view that works over SSH: tenant profiles or the sample tenant, the assessment and its score, findings, the Path analyzer for one destination, Test a list from a file, snapshots and their differences, and plan previews. It is a client of the running server, so writes go only through the same plan, typed confirmation and apply path with the same gates; the UI token is never shown, secrets are masked and terminal escape sequences from tenant strings are stripped.
- **Local plugins.** Off by default: Python packages in the folder `DLP_CHECK_PLUGINS_DIR` names load only when switched on in Advanced settings and only when an admin approved the package's exact SHA-256 there (shown with its name, version and the permissions it asks for, the name typed to confirm, **Approve this hash**). A plugin can add Bulk changes operations, planned with a read-only client and written only through the engine's gates, and read-only pages shown as text; an approval can name the people allowed to use it. A modified or unlisted plugin is refused and never run. SECURITY.md states the cost plainly; an example plugin lives in docs/plugins (not loaded).
- **Update notifications.** When the update check finds a newer version, the app can say so once per version on the e-mail or webhook channel the digests use. Off by default; switched in Advanced settings, App version and updates.

### Network files: DNS records for client hostnames and Zscaler IP range allowlists
- **DNS records for client hostnames** (Client Connector fleet). For ZPA client-to-client access, each enrolled device gets one DNS name, `<hostname>.<domain>`, sent to the client-to-client resolver you name (a Branch Connector, for example). **Build the records** builds it from the fleet read on the page (read-only, only with ZCC ticked on the Scope page): no second tenant read, no DNS server contacted, nothing applied. Downloads: Unbound forward zones (with `local-zone ... transparent` and `domain-insecure` lines, each switchable), BIND forward zones for named.conf, a BIND zone file part that delegates each name to the resolver, and a CSV of every device with its name and what happened to it. Hostnames are rebuilt as RFC 1123 labels (first label only, accents folded, lower case, other characters become '-', at most 63 characters), so no quote, space, ';' or newline reaches a configuration file; renames, duplicate enrolments and collisions (two hostnames that give the same name) are listed and each name is written once. Options: platforms, registered only, seen in the last N days, e-mail domain to DNS domain mappings (a second name per device) and the zone file TTL. Hostnames are never logged; the audit record holds counts.
- **Zscaler IP ranges and allowlists** (Policy exports). Zscaler's published ranges and host names for one ZIA cloud (cloud enforcement nodes by region and city, hub ranges recommended and required, future data centres, App Connector tunnel addresses) and optionally a ZPA cloud's allow list, aggregated by **Build the allowlist** into the fewest CIDRs per region and in all (IPv4 and IPv6 apart), with ranges you leave out (a larger range is split around them) and ranges you add. Downloads: a plain list, CSV, Palo Alto external dynamic lists (IP and URL), JSON with every source and its date, and a Microsoft 365-style endpoint list. Each source shows when it was fetched or imported and what changed since the copy before; a feed not kept is unknown, never empty, and one older than 30 days is stale. **Fetch from config.zscaler.com** is off by default and, when on, fetches only when you click **Fetch now** (GET only, that one host, no redirect followed, 10-second timeout, at most 4 MB a feed, nothing about the tenant sent); **Import** takes the same JSON saved by hand for offline installs; **Forget the kept feeds** removes them. Every feed is checked as untrusted: a wrong shape is refused whole and the copy kept before stays; private, reserved or over-broad ranges (wider than /8 or /16) and host names outside Zscaler's domains are dropped and counted. `config.zscaler.com` is listed with the app's other outbound destinations in SECURITY.md. The feed paths and field names follow Zscaler's config portal and are marked as assumed in the code until checked against a live answer.

### Wave 7: closing the gaps a comparison with other Zscaler tools found
- **Client Connector bypasses on the Path analyzer.** The Client Connector step checks, in order, the app profile's **VPN gateway bypass**, its host name and address bypasses, its **IP-based application bypass** (the predefined and custom IP-based applications and app services the profile names, each read from the tenant with its addresses, ports and protocols: SDK 1.11.3 `custom-ip-based-apps`, `predefined-ip-based-apps`), the application bypass by name, then the forwarding mode for the protocol and port traced (**Z-Tunnel 1.0 and 2.0**): Z-Tunnel 1.0 (and Tunnel with local proxy) carries web traffic on TCP 80 and 443 only, Enforce proxy carries web traffic only, Z-Tunnel 2.0 carries every port and protocol except its own destination exclusions (and only its inclusions when they are limited), then the PAC file (evaluated, never run). The step is drawn like every other module, with each list as a criterion (match, no match, unknown) and the list and entry that decided as its rule. A definition that could not be read, or a host name that limited inclusions would need resolved, is unknown, never "no bypass". Test a list relates its rows to the new lists too.
- **Tamper protection passwords.** The Client Connector profile builder can set the new app profile's uninstall, turn-off (Internet security) and log-out passwords. They are typed in password fields, sent beside the operation (never in it) for the preview and the apply, written once in the create call and never kept: not in the plan, the plan PDF, the record, the audit log, job logs, snapshots, exports or the browser's storage. The plan shows "set (hidden)" and its hash covers only a salted, keyed digest, so a different password typed at apply stops the write; every error is masked; the read-back says set or not set; the preview says that Undo deletes the profiles but cannot restore a password, because none is kept.
- **One-time passwords for a device.** The Client Connector fleet page shows one device's one-time passwords (uninstall, turn a service off, exit, as the tenant provides them: SDK 1.11.3 `getOtp`) for the help desk: ZCC ticked on the Scope page, a person allowed to change the tenant, the assessor's name and SHOW OTP and the device typed, a **rate limit** of 5 requests in 10 minutes per person. They are shown once, with a Copy button, and hidden after 60 seconds; never stored, logged or cached. The audit log records who asked, when and for which device (host name and the fleet page's short UDID hash), never a password.

### Path analyzer: a user's device, two identities side by side, and a test from this computer
- **Trace a user's device.** Under Device on the Path analyzer, type a user's sign-in name and Find devices: the Client Connector Portal is asked once (getDevices filtered by user name, at most 20 shown, only devices whose user matches), and only with Client Connector ticked on the Scope page and a OneAPI sign-in. Pick one (one getDeviceDetails call) and the trace uses that device's platform, the app profile the Portal reports for it (instead of choosing one by platform, user and group), its Client Connector version and, when the API gives them, whether ZIA and ZPA are on, its device trust level and strict enforcement. The result says which app and forwarding profile applied and why, lists every fact with where it came from, and says "unknown" for what the API does not give: the network the device is on right now (the Network you choose applies) and its ZPA posture results. ZIA turned off on the device, an unsupported client and a device that is not registered are named as impacts. The device's UDID stays on the server; the page gets a hash.
- **Compare two identities.** The same destination, network and traffic for identity A (the what-if and the device) and identity B (user, group, department, location and a device of their own), on one tenant read: every step side by side, the step where the paths diverge marked in words, and CSV (spreadsheet-safe) and JSON downloads (`POST /api/path-analyzer/compare/start`, `GET /api/path-analyzer/compare/{job_id}/export`).
- **Test from here.** After a result, one explicit click tests the destination from the computer running this app (in Docker, the container's network): a name lookup, a TCP connect and, on a TLS port, a TLS handshake (issuer, subject and names on the certificate, TLS version, whether this computer trusts it), compared with the prediction, for example "predicted: inspected by ZIA; observed: certificate issued by Zscaler Intermediate Root CA". It sends no HTTP request, data or credentials and follows no redirect; ports 443, 80, 8443 and 8080 only; loopback, link-local (cloud metadata), multicast and reserved addresses refused; at most 6 a minute, one at a time; nothing kept. SECURITY.md has its threat model; `ZSCALER_TEST_FROM_HERE=0` turns it off.

### Wave 7: closing the gaps a comparison with other Zscaler tools found
- **Fix rows from Test a list.** *Fix selected* on the Path analyzer's Test a list view no longer stops at URL categories: each picked row is fixed for what it was expected to do (block or allow), in the area its recommendation names. URL filtering and Cloud App Control rows still fill the URL category change; Cloud Firewall and DNS Control rows go into IP destination groups (IPv4 addresses, CIDR blocks and ranges in a group of type IP, single addresses merged into blocks; host names in a group of type FQDN; an existing group or a new one), then onto an existing Block or Allow rule (with network services, for the Cloud Firewall) or a new rule, created disabled unless you tick it on, placed above the rule that decided the rows; ATP rows go onto the security policy denylist or allowlist, the lists the Path analyzer's ATP step reads. Rows that ZPA or Client Connector decide, rows already as expected and parts not ticked on the Scope page are left out with the reason. The preview's **What this changes, in plain words** says each change in a sentence and links to the rows it fixes in the **Rows this fixes** table, and warns when a rule sits below the one that decided the rows or is disabled. The usual engine: the Scope page checked before any read, preview with a plan hash, write mode, a writer, the assessor's name and the typed phrase (always, for a change that acts at activation: a rule, the ATP lists, a group an enabled rule uses), a re-read of every object right before its write (anything changed meanwhile is left as it is), one activation, read-back, audit and an Undo that deletes what it created only while it is unchanged. Limits: 8,000 entries per group and 16,000 IP entries per organisation (assumed from Zscaler's ranges page; a new group past the limit is split into numbered groups, an existing one refuses the rest), 31 characters per rule name (SDK 1.11.3), no IPv6 entries (the API has no user-defined IPv6 destination group).
- **Prove it.** After the change is applied, *Prove it* tests the same rows again with Test a list (fresh, only what is in scope) and says, row by row, which now do what was expected, with the rule that decides them now; a new rule left disabled is named as the reason rows are not fixed yet.
- **IP destination group change** and **ATP allow and deny lists** are also Bulk changes operations of their own, without Test a list: add or remove entries on one IP destination group (or make a new one), or URLs on the security policy denylist or allowlist, with the same limits, gates, re-read, read-back and Undo.

### A saved profile's cloud is never replaced by a default
- **Fix.** The request bodies behind the Path analyzer, data flow, migration, flow scenario, assessment and remediation routes no longer default their cloud to "production": a request naming only a saved profile signs in on the profile's own cloud, also when the request is rebuilt from every field, and production still applies when nothing (typed, profile, environment or `tenant_config.json`) names one. The assessment report names the cloud it signed in on. The POV planner's *Prove it* no longer needs its own workaround. A test now fails when a request model that takes a profile gets a cloud default again.

### Team server: several people on one server, each with their own access (optional, off by default)
- **Team server.** `ZSCALER_TEAM_MODE=1` turns the app into a multi-user server; without it nothing changes (one person, this computer, the UI token). Each person signs in with their own account (passwords hashed with scrypt, or PBKDF2 in FIPS mode), or through the existing OpenID Connect SSO matched to their account. Roles: viewer (read), operator (read, plan, apply), approver (read, approve), administrator. An administrator gives read, plan, apply or approve per tenant profile to a person or a group; nothing is allowed until it is given. Every route has an access rule (`team_routes.py`), checked on every request: a route with no rule is refused, and a test lists every route and fails on one without a rule. A tenant is reached only through a tenant profile the person may use; reports, jobs, saved plans, change requests and records belong to their tenant profile; lists show only the person's tenant profiles.
- **Sign in.** A sign-in page (`/login`): five wrong passwords lock the account for 15 minutes (enforced, even with the right password), sign-in attempts are limited per address, a new session id at every sign-in, the cookie HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict and Secure over HTTPS, sessions end after 15 quiet minutes and after 8 hours, and a change that carries the cookie must come from this server's own page.
- **Still there?** Every page warns two minutes before an idle sign-out (SSO and team server), with Stay signed in, and five minutes before a session's absolute end.
- **SCIM 2.0** at `/api/scim/v2` (team server only): your identity provider adds, changes and removes accounts and groups with a bearer token an administrator makes on the Team server page; it is shown once, kept only as a hash, and cannot make an administrator.
- **HTTPS built in.** Your certificate, a self-signed one made on the first start (its fingerprint printed), or Let's Encrypt through the dns-01 challenge on Cloudflare (`ZSCALER_TLS=acme`; the API token in the OS credential store; the only new outbound calls, and only when turned on). An optional plain-HTTP port only redirects to HTTPS; HSTS on a team server. A team server reachable beyond this computer does not start without HTTPS; the older ways beyond loopback (SSO, a reverse proxy) still start, with a loud warning.
- **Four-eyes with real people.** On a team server the approver must be another signed-in person with approve on that tenant profile; `DLP_CHECK_FOUR_EYES=0` cannot turn it off. Every audit entry names its person and tenant profile, and a person sees only their tenant profiles' entries.
- **Shared templates and saved plans.** POV templates and saved deploy plans belong to who saved them; they share them with people and groups (a shared plan still opens only for people who may read its tenant profile).
- **Encryption at rest and FIPS mode** (any mode, off by default). `DLP_CHECK_STORE_ENCRYPTION=1` seals every local store (profiles, snapshots, change queue, proposals, deployment records, notes, POVs, accounts, the audit log) with AES-256-GCM, the key in the OS credential store or an owner-only key file; `python store_crypto.py rotate` re-seals all of them with a new key. `DLP_CHECK_FIPS_MODE=1` keeps the app's own choices to FIPS-approved algorithms; it does not make Python a validated module (SECURITY.md says what it covers).
- **Not built in.** SAML (it needs an XML-signature library that is not in the pinned dependencies) and passkeys (no vetted WebAuthn library is pinned); use the identity provider's OpenID Connect app. INSTALL.md and SECURITY.md have a Team server section with the threat model.

### Export the whole tenant as Terraform
- **Export from tenant as Terraform (whole tenant)**, on Policy exports: every object in scope on the Scope page as one Terraform workspace per product for the official providers, `zscaler/zia` (~> 4.8, 58 resource types: settings, Advanced Threat and Malware Protection, URL categories and URL filtering, SSL inspection, Cloud App Control, File Type Control, Cloud Firewall, DNS and IPS Control, NAT, Sandbox, bandwidth, forwarding and ZPA gateways, IP, network service and application groups, rule labels, locations and traffic forwarding, DLP dictionaries, engines, templates and rules on every channel), `zscaler/zpa` (~> 4.4, 31: segments of every kind including Browser Access, PRA and inspection, segment and server groups, servers, connector and Service Edge groups, provisioning keys, the access, timeout, forwarding, inspection and isolation policies with their conditions, PRA, portals, isolation, LSS, microtenants), `zscaler/ztc` (~> 0.2, 10 Cloud & Branch Connector types) and `zscaler/zcc` (~> 0.1, trusted networks and forwarding profiles). Works on the live tenant, the sample tenant and an opened snapshot; read-only.
- **Adopt or clone.** Adopt (the default) writes an `import` block (Terraform 1.5+) for every object and keeps each object's state, so the first apply brings the tenant into Terraform state without recreating or changing anything. Clone writes new objects for another tenant: no import blocks, every rule DISABLED and every ZPA application segment disabled unless *Keep each rule's state* is ticked, settings and objects bound to this tenant (locations, tunnels, connectors, cloud accounts) left out with the reason.
- **References, not ids.** A reference to another exported object is its Terraform address; one that is not exported (a predefined network service, dictionary or engine, a group, a department) is a data source looked up by name; a user keeps its id and is never named. A DLP engine's expression names its dictionaries by address inside the string.
- **No secrets, no surprises.** Every secret-named field is masked as it is read; pre-shared keys, PRA passwords, passphrases and private keys become sensitive map variables the operator fills in. Users and admins are never exported; alert subscriptions and PRA approvals (email addresses) only when asked. Each workspace has typed, described variables, OneAPI credentials from variables or the environment, a provider pin, a commented S3 backend, outputs, a README and `.gitignore`; files are `terraform fmt`-clean; every zip path is made by the app. What was not exported, and every argument left out, is listed with the reason. Options: products, object types, a resource-label prefix, a private registry namespace, a ZPA microtenant.
- API: `GET /api/tenant-terraform/catalogue`, `POST /api/tenant-terraform/start` (a job; poll and download through `/api/dlp-packs/jobs/...`). Anything out of scope is a 400 before sign-in. CI checks the sample tenant's export with `terraform fmt -check` and (advisory) `terraform validate`.

### Client Connector fleet: versions, profiles, last seen, sites, history, CSV import and a PDF report
- **Service health by version.** The fleet page shows, per platform and Client Connector version, the registered devices and the share with ZIA, ZPA and ZDX active (among devices whose status is known; not applicable and unreadable are left out). A version 10 or more points below the whole fleet, with at least 5 devices known, is flagged "below fleet"; the newest and unsupported releases are marked. A Quarantined tile joins the fleet tiles.
- **Health per app and forwarding profile.** The same shares per app profile (the policy name each device reports, per platform) and, through the forwarding profile each read app profile links to, per forwarding profile, with devices seen in 24 hours and ghosts.
- **Last-seen histogram.** Registered devices by how long before the read they were last seen: under 1 day, 1 to 7 days, 7 to 30 days, over 30 days and never, to the second, with the exact definition on the page.
- **Site grouping.** Devices counted per site from their host names: the first 1 to 12 characters, the part before the first to fourth hyphen, underscore or dot, or a list of up to 200 site prefixes (the longest match wins). Never a pattern the operator types, so nothing can hang the server. The choice is kept in the browser and used by the exports; a counts-only export merges sites with fewer than 3 registered devices.
- **Fleet history and trends.** Save a read (or an import) to the history: a counts-only snapshot (totals, ZIA, ZPA and ZDX, alerts, last seen, the versions in use; never a user, host name, device ID or site) kept per tenant on this computer in an owner-only file, 60 per tenant. The page draws the trend, shows each read's change since the one before and compares any two reads; removing a tenant's history asks first. The file is read back as untrusted.
- **Import the portal's CSV exports.** Without the Client Connector device permission, import the portal's Device Details and Service Status exports (Enrolled Devices, Export) and get the same figures without a tenant sign-in. The files are checked as untrusted: 48 MB each (compressed in the browser and inflated through a bounded read), 200,000 rows, 300 columns, 200 characters a cell, a NUL byte or a damaged upload refused, the header found by its column names. The export time comes from the portal's file names; two files exported more than 12 hours apart are flagged.
- **Fleet PDF report.** The Report (PDF) button writes the page as a PDF (counts only: no user or device is named), and the Excel workbook gains Version health, Profile health, Forwarding health, Last seen, Sites and History sheets.
- The service status file's "ZIA Health" column is now preferred over "ZIA Enabled" when both are present, and a service that is not enabled for a device counts as not applicable. The sample tenant has a service status file, so its fleet shows ZIA, ZPA and ZDX (the contractor laptop is a bypass risk).

### Wave 7: closing the gaps a comparison with other Zscaler tools found
- **Traffic and session logs.** A new page (Troubleshoot → Traffic and session logs, `/report/traffic-logs`) reads the logs a customer's log streaming already writes, on this computer only: ZPA LSS User Activity, User Status, App Connector Status and Private Service Edge Status (JSON lines), and ZIA NSS web, firewall and DNS feeds (JSON, the Cloud NSS HTTP shape, a CSV or TSV with a header row in NSS or Insights column names, or the default tab-separated format). It shows sessions and failures over time; the top users, applications, App Connectors, Service Edges, access rules and destinations; every ZPA internal reason with what it means and what to do; ZPA sessions put together per user (sign-in checks under 5 seconds left out, session ID rotation within 60 seconds merged); and for ZIA blocked against allowed, top categories, rules hit per module, DLP and threat events and SSL inspected or not. A destination opens in the Path analyzer (filled in, never started), and Traffic insights opens with the same window; Traffic insights links back for what Insights cannot answer.
- **Connector and Service Edge health** from the App Connector and Private Service Edge Status logs: down or failing at the end of the logs, flapping (3 disconnects or more), failed sign-ins, CPU or memory at 85% or more, and groups running more than one version.
- **Anomalies**: spikes (a time slot at least 3 times the median and at least 10), users with many failures (10 that are half their connections, or 25), destinations first seen in the last day of the logs, and connector problems.
- **Custom NSS feed formats.** Paste a feed's output format (`%s{time}\t%s{login}...`) and lines that are not JSON are split by it; each field stops at the next separator, so a hostile line cannot make the reader backtrack.
- **Untrusted input, in memory only.** Files go through the Log analyzer's bounded unpacking, then are read line by line: lines over 64 KB and lines past 2,000,000 are counted and skipped, every counter stops taking new values at 50,000, and a line no format reads is counted as malformed and never stops the run. Only counts are kept, in the server's memory, until you forget them.
- **Counts only** downloads by default: CSV, Excel and PDF with the app's exporters show users as user 1, user 2 and leave the session list out unless you tick to include names.
- **Saved reports.** Save writes an owner-only file on this computer (counts only unless you tick names) and an audit line with no user names; the page lists, opens and deletes them, and reads a saved file as untrusted (name pattern, size, format).
- **SIEM queries.** The same questions as Splunk SPL, Microsoft Sentinel KQL and Elastic ES|QL text to copy, for the NSS and LSS field names, filtered to a user or a destination if you like (each value escaped for the query language, each index or table name checked).

### Tenant health, API call diagnostics and ZDX probes fitted to the tier
- **Readiness: pending activation and SSL rule order.** Right after Authenticate, readiness also reads ZIA's activation status (`zia.activate.status()`, GET `/status`) and, when DLP or ZIA platform is ticked, the SSL inspection rules. It says, with plain steps, when changes are saved but not activated (a deploy from this app activates them too) and when the rule order stops rules from applying: a broad Do Not Inspect rule above Inspect rules, a broad Inspect rule above the exemptions (Zscaler Recommended Exemptions included), or nothing inspecting what reaches the Default rule. Shown on Start and on Required API access.
- **Tenant health** (on the FP/TP detection ratio page, `/report/health`): ZIA activation, the SSL rule order, certificate expiry (ZIA SSL inspection intermediate CA certificates, ZPA enrollment certificates with the connectors and service edges enrolled with each, ZPA web server and client signing certificates) with **certificate expiry thresholds** you set: a warning and a critical one (30 and 7 days; `DLP_CHECK_CERT_WARN_DAYS`, `DLP_CHECK_CERT_CRIT_DAYS`, or per check), and App Connector groups with no connector. Read-only, only what the Scope page ticks. Four new assessment checks carry the same signals: HLT.1 to HLT.4.
- **API calls** (Advanced settings): every Zscaler API call this app made since it started, with the time, product, method, the path with every id shown as `{id}`, the answer, the time taken, how often it was sent again and how long it waited for a rate limit, and an error class. Never a body, header, token, secret, query or tenant name. Kept in memory only (the newest 1,000, `DLP_CHECK_API_CALLS_MAX`), empty after a restart; filter by run or product; **export as CSV or JSON**. A builder's result links to the **API calls of this run**.
- **ZDX probes fitted to the tier.** ZDX probe setup now starts from a tier (the ZIA security baseline's): Strict monitors the most apps every 5 minutes with CloudPath probes, Balanced the Microsoft 365 core every 10 minutes, Permissive sign-in only every 15 minutes with Web probes, Healthcare adds Zoom every 5 minutes. Anything looser than the tier is a warning in the preview. What is verified against SDK 1.11.3 (the `/zdx/v1` prefix, the request routing and the bodies sent as given) is now said apart from what stays assumed (the configuration API itself, which the SDK does not have).

### POV build and rehearsal on the sample tenant and snapshots
- **POV build: one plan, one dry run, one apply.** After the intake the POV planner builds the tenant the POV needs through the Build new builders (`pov_build.py`): the ZIA security baseline, SSL certificates, the ZPA App Connector group, application segments and policy rules, Client Connector profiles, ZDX probes, AI Guard routing and its detection policy, each with its safe defaults for the vertical preset. New objects arrive disabled or monitor-only unless you choose otherwise. A preset's builder defaults are data (`pov_build.PRESET_DEFAULTS`, or the preset's own `build` entry), so a new intake preset needs no code.
- **Build tab.** On the POV planner: tick the parts (a part whose product is not ticked on the Scope page cannot be picked, and the server refuses it before sign-in), change the main options, or the part exactly as it is sent.
- **One dry run.** Every part planned by its own builder in a safe order, what other parts use first (the connector group before the segments, the segments before the policy rules: a part that needs an earlier part's object is planned against it instead of being blocked), shown as a section per product with every object before and after, under one plan hash over every part.
- **One typed confirmation.** `APPLY <n> POV CHANGES` applies the whole build. Every gate stays: write mode, a writer, the named assessor, the Scope rule per part, the ZPA customer ID. The whole plan is read again first (a tenant that moved writes nothing); then each part is read again right before it is written and must match the preview, and is applied through its builder's own checks (its plan hash and phrase, a re-read before every write, read-back, activation, the audit log) and recorded with its Undo. The first part that does not apply completely stops the run, and the report names it and what was applied. Each build is listed on the POV.
- **Undo everything applied so far.** The recorded Undos of a build's applied parts, latest first, as one dry run and one confirmation (`UNDO <n> POV CHANGES`), stopping at the first that does not apply.
- **Load choices from the tenant.** The SSL certificates and AI Guard routing parts read the certificates and Inspect rules to pick from (read-only, only what is in scope).
- **Rehearsal on the sample tenant and snapshots.** Every Build new type, the classic Bulk changes types and the POV build now make their full plan and dry run on the sample tenant and on an opened snapshot, labelled "Rehearsal — nothing was written". The sample carries the catalogues the builders read (URL categories, network and cloud applications, the Trusted Resolver, intermediate and AI Guard root certificates, ZPA enrollment certificates and OWASP controls, the ZDX configuration, AI Guard's lists) and an Inspect rule to move; a saved snapshot now keeps those lists for the products in scope. An apply there (a bulk change, a builder, a POV build, a pack deploy, removal or Undo) is refused with a plain message before any client is built, so nothing is written to the sample's memory either. This closes the open finding that most Build new types could not plan on the sample.
- **Rehearsal in Deploy policy packs.** A pack's preview (and the removal preview) on the sample tenant or a snapshot says it is a rehearsal; deploying there is refused the same way.

### Bulk ZPA segment edits, a larger URL category analyzer, ANY in packs
- **ZPA segment settings in bulk.** A new Bulk changes operation (`ops_zpa_fields.py`, *ZPA segment settings in bulk*, next to the four existing operations) sets the same fields on up to 200 application segments at once: enabled, health reporting (none, on access, continuous), bypass (never, always, on trusted network), double encryption, disaster recovery mode, passive health checks, ICMP access, the segment group, **server groups and port ranges** (server groups added, removed or replaced; TCP / UDP ranges added or removed). **Browser Access, PRA and inspection segments** are included: ZPA's update replaces the segment, so each write sends it back as read right before it with only the chosen fields changed, Browser Access applications included; the read-back checks every Browser Access, PRA and inspection application is still there. The preview keeps the last server group, the last port range and the port a Browser Access, PRA or inspection application uses, refuses a port that would put one domain in two segments on overlapping ports, and skips read-only segments, each with the reason. The usual engine: Scope page (ZPA ticked), plan hash, the four write gates, a re-read before each write (a field changed since the preview is a conflict and left alone), read-back, an **exact Undo** and the audit log.
- **Continue in the URL category analyzer.** The custom URL category analyzer now looks up **20,000 URLs a run** (it was 2,000; 200 requests, about four minutes at one a second, under ZIA's assumed 400 POST requests an hour) and shows how far it is (**progress and a stop on rate limits**). When URLs are left (the per-run cap, a rate limit ZIA keeps answering after the retries, which now ends the run instead of sending more, or Stop), what was looked up is kept in the app's memory and Continue looks up only the rest; the workbook of each run covers everything so far. The checkpoint is bound to who started it, the tenant and the categories picked, at most four are kept for six hours with 200,000 URLs in all, and a job result carries only its token. One analysis covers up to 100,000 distinct URLs; any beyond that are counted and named.
- Policy packs: a literal `["ANY"]` in a rule's (or sub-rule's) `device_trust_levels` or `user_risk_score_levels` means every level and is expanded to the explicit list when the pack is read, so the in-app deploy and the Terraform workspace both send what the API and the zscaler/zia provider take; `ANY` next to other levels, or an unknown level, is refused with the reason (pack library check, preflight, the pack reader).
- Docs: `docs/COMPARISON_zbp-automation.md` is refreshed against what the app does now (generated Terraform for every pack, Endpoint DLP sub-rules and application groups, lifecycle, CI). **Why the Endpoint DLP pack is gone**: the retired ZBP Endpoint DLP pack (2026-09-11) and its replacement (every pack mirrors its rules onto Endpoint DLP) are explained on the pack library page (Advanced settings) and in POLICY_PACKS.md.
- **Verified.** New tests in `tests/test_ops_zpa_fields.py` (a fake ZPA whose update replaces the segment, and the made-up sample tenant through the installed SDK), `tests/test_url_analyzer_resume.py` and `tests/test_pack_any_levels.py`.

### ZPA policy completeness
- **Every ZPA policy type is read.** ZPA analysis (and Device posture, which shares the read) now reads the redirection, privileged credential, privileged capabilities and browser protection (clientless session protection) policies beside the access, timeout, client forwarding, inspection and isolation policies, plus AppProtection inspection profiles and custom controls, application servers, business continuity settings, Private Cloud sites, Private Cloud Controller groups and controllers, and Branch and Cloud Connector groups and connectors (`zpa_reader.py`; every SDK class and method checked against SDK 1.11.3). Read-only, only when ZPA is ticked on the Scope page. Business continuity carries SAML private keys and certificates: only its switches and timers are kept. The policy-group model is left out until it is documented.
- **AppProtection inspection.** Web segments with no INSPECT rule at all (ZPA-IN-01), inspection profiles whose every control only passes and logs (ZPA-IN-02, medium when a rule uses it) and custom controls no profile uses (ZPA-IN-03).
- **Client forwarding bypass.** A BYPASS forwarding rule that decides for a sensitive segment (directory, database or remote admin ports, a Privileged Remote Access segment, or a name such as finance, payroll, HR or production): high for everyone, medium for some users (ZPA-CF-01).
- **Privileged Remote Access.** Capabilities rules that allow clipboard copy or file transfer without ZIA sandbox inspection (ZPA-PR-01), PRA segments whose sessions are never recorded (ZPA-PR-02) and stored credentials given with no user condition (ZPA-PR-03).
- **Redirection.** REDIRECT_ALWAYS or REDIRECT_PREFERRED rules whose Private Service Edge groups are missing, disabled, empty or disconnected (ZPA-RD-01).
- **Unmanaged-device access.** Browser Access segments allowed with no isolation rule (ZPA-IS-01, high when sensitive) or no browser protection MONITOR rule (ZPA-BP-01).
- **Infrastructure in the diagnostics.** Application servers in no server group (ZPA-SV-03), Private Cloud Controller groups not serving (ZPA-PC-01), business continuity with nothing to fail over to (ZPA-BC-01) and empty Branch or Cloud Connector groups (ZPA-EC-01). Diagnose now names the first rule of every policy type, counts a server group's servers from the server list, and adds a Branch or Cloud Connector hop for those client types and a business continuity hop.
- **Six new assessment checks.** ZPA.16 to ZPA.21 (inspection, client forwarding, PRA, redirection, unmanaged-device access, business continuity) run the same templates over the assessment's own reads; a 404 is N/A (not licensed), and PRA, Browser Access or business continuity not in use is N/A. Snapshots capture every new list, the sample tenant carries a gap for each, and the Scope page lists the new endpoints. The Path analyzer and Device posture skip the new lists, so their reads are no longer than before. 23 new tests in tests/test_zpa_policy_completeness.py.

### ZIA forwarding and traffic path
- **Forwarding and traffic path checks.** Ten new ZIA platform checks (section "ZIA — Forwarding", FWD.1 to FWD.10), read only when ZIA platform is ticked. A feature that is not licensed (the API answers 404) or not used is "not applicable" and leaves the score alone; each finding says why it matters and how to fix it.
  - **Forwarding around ZIA**: Cloud or Branch Connector forwarding rules that send traffic DIRECT, with no destination named (fail) or for named destinations (review).
  - **Proxy chaining fallback**: proxy-chaining rules with no proxy gateway, or a gateway with no secondary proxy (fail when it also does not fail closed).
  - **ZPA gateways**: no server group or application segment, ZPA rules with no gateway, gateways no rule uses.
  - **Data centre exclusions**: exclusions in force, open-ended or longer than 30 days.
  - **GRE tunnel diversity**: a secondary VIP that is missing or in the primary's data centre (a VIP the tunnel does not place is placed from Zscaler's public VIP list).
  - **IPv6 handling**: IPv6 off while locations forward traffic or are set to carry IPv6; IPv6 on with no NAT64 prefix.
  - **Dedicated IP gateway failover**: no secondary data centre, or the same one as the primary.
  - **NAT control scope**: NAT control rules with no destination named or no redirect target.
  - **Virtual Service Edge redundancy**: clusters with fewer than two working nodes, nodes in no cluster, nodes off or not in production.
  - **DNS gateway failover**: no secondary resolver (fail when the gateway also answers with an error).
- **Forwarding control step.** The Path analyzer's last ZIA step: the ZIA forwarding rule that decides how allowed traffic is sent on (direct, proxy chaining, ZPA, a dedicated egress IP, or dropped, which ends the path as blocked), every criterion as match, no match or unknown, and what the method relies on: the proxy gateway's fallback, the ZPA gateway's server group, the dedicated IP gateway's data centres. It also names a NAT control rule that rewrites (or could rewrite) the destination, IPv6 being off for an IPv6 destination, and data centre exclusions in force; a DNS Control rule that redirects to a DNS gateway says whether that gateway can fail over. A weak point (no fallback proxy, no ZPA gateway, IPv6 not processed) is listed with its fix. Not licensed: the step says "not applicable" and changes nothing else.
- The sample tenant (Larkspur Freight) has forwarding rules, gateways, GRE tunnels and VIPs, data centres and an exclusion, IPv6, NAT control, a Virtual Service Edge cluster and a DNS gateway, with gaps to find; snapshots save the same reads. Read-only: nothing here writes to the tenant.
- Advanced settings at 320px: the "Add a pack" fields and buttons no longer run past the edge of the screen (the column could not shrink below its widest input), so the help mark beside "Add to library" stays on screen.

### ZIA threat protection and SaaS Security hygiene
