- **Threat protection checks.** Eleven new scored checks under ZIA platform (section ZIA — Security): the Cloud Sandbox custom file hash list (no allow-listed hash skips the sandbox; the quota is not nearly full), custom IPS signatures (none rejected by validation or disabled), mobile malware protection (malicious, vulnerable and clear-text-credential apps blocked), FTP control (native FTP not open to every site), end-user notifications (a reason, a support contact, the policy link and review requests), security alerts (UEBA rules on), custom file types, Browser Control, domain fronting blocked, cloud app risk profiles used by a Cloud App Control rule, and adaptive access profiles. Each says what was found, why it matters and how to fix it.
- **URL and app settings.** SafeSearch, newly registered domains with AI/ML content categorisation and translated sites, and Microsoft 365 one-click configuration are now scored (ZIA — URL Filtering, UF.5 to UF.7); before, they were only read by other pages. **Browser Control** is scored too (THR.8).
- **SaaS Security hygiene.** Four checks in the new section ZIA — SaaS Security: SaaS tenants with no enabled malware rule (named), malware rules that only report or quarantine without a tombstone, tenants that need re-authorising or show no scan, and cloud-to-cloud incident receivers that failed validation. The malware rules, tenant scan state and quarantine templates are read only when SaaS Security is marked Zscaler on the Scope page; the receivers are read from the lite list, never the one that holds tenant credentials.
- **Not licensed is not a failure.** A feature the tenant does not have (the API answers 404: Advanced Cloud Firewall, Mobile Malware Protection, SaaS Security API, Cloud Sandbox, adaptive access) is reported as not licensed, outside the score; a refused read stays "Not permitted". Required API access lists the new permissions under ZIA platform.
- **Sandbox file hashes.** A new Bulk changes builder adds the MD5 hashes of files your incident response confirmed as malicious to the Cloud Sandbox custom block list: up to 100 per operation, each checked as an MD5 (a SHA-256 is refused with the reason), with an optional comment. It goes through the same engine as every write: preview with the plan hash, write mode, a writer, the named assessor and, always, a typed phrase (BLOCK n FILE HASHES); the list is read again right before the write and sent back with only these hashes added (an allow-listed hash is never touched), read back after activation, and Undo removes exactly the hashes that were added.
- **Offline too.** Every new read is in the sample tenant (with gaps to find: native FTP open, SafeSearch off, an unused risk profile, two SaaS tenants without a malware rule) and in saved snapshots; the sample models the hash list write in memory.

### Shadow IT sanction and Insights checks
- **High-risk unsanctioned apps still carry traffic** (check INS.1), **High-risk apps with no sanction decision** (INS.2) and **Sanctioned SaaS apps with no SaaS Security API tenant** (INS.3): three assessment checks from ZIA Insights' Shadow IT applications of the last 30 days (aggregates per application: name, risk index, sanction state, bytes), with a plain finding, why it matters and the fix. They run only when ZIA Insights is ticked on the Scope page (never under the legacy full scope) and say N/A when there is no traffic, no CASB subscription (INS.3) or the SaaS channel is not on Zscaler. Each uses the SDK's own Z-Insights query, sent through the assessment client.
- **Sanction or unsanction cloud apps** in Bulk changes: up to 100 cloud applications marked Sanctioned or Unsanctioned in one reviewed plan, through `shadow_it_report.bulk_update`. The change is tenant-wide, so the typed phrase is always asked; each app's state is read again right before the write and an app that moved is skipped. **Exact Undo**: every app goes back to the state the plan read (from ZIA Insights, matched to the app list from `list_apps`); an app whose state cannot be read is not changed. Needs ZIA platform and ZIA Insights ticked. The old "sanction is not offered" note now points to it.
- Traffic insights gains **Web threats by class and super-category**, **Firewall traffic by location and network service**, **Security incidents by location** and **IoT and unmanaged devices** (device counts now; the IoT query takes no time window). Aggregates only, in the CSV and JSON exports too.
- The sample tenant answers every Insights question, the cloud application list and the licences, with gaps the new checks find; a snapshot keeps the Insights answers per question (last 30 days, never the per-user one) and the cloud application list.

## 2026-10-30

### The new name everywhere: Docker, downloads, commands and the install guide
- **Docker names follow the app's name.** Container and compose service `zscaler-made-easy`, image `zscaler-made-easy:local`, data volume `zscaler-made-easy-data` (a fixed name, no longer tied to the folder name), compose project `zscaler-made-easy`. Upgrading loses nothing: the first `update_container.ps1` / `update_container.sh` run after this copies the old `dlpcheck-data` / `<folder>_dlpcheck-data` volume into the new one (owners, modes and timestamps kept, checked file by file), removes the old container so port 8001 is free, keeps the old volume as a backup and prints the command to remove it; later runs copy nothing; a failed copy stops before the new container starts. INSTALL.md, *Moving to the new name*, has the same steps by hand for Docker-only installs.
- **Downloads and commands.** The browser extension downloads as `zscaler-made-easy-extension.zip`, Terraform downloads start with `zscaler-made-easy-`, and the commands are `zscaler-made-easy`, `zscaler-made-easy-assess` and `zscaler-made-easy-evaluate` (the `dlp-check` names still work). The in-app guide clones `berserkerjay/Zscaler-Made-Easy`; INSTALL.md uses the new folder names (`Zscaler-Made-Easy`, `Zscaler-Made-Easy-main`); the SBOM licence id is `LicenseRef-Zscaler-Made-Easy-Internal`.
- **Unchanged on purpose.** `DLP_CHECK_*` settings, the `X-DLP-Check-*` headers, the `[DLP-Check …]` tags on tenant objects, cookie and browser-storage keys: they are what existing installs and tenants already use. A test now fails CI when an old name appears anywhere else.
- **Marks on new features.** A `*` after the name of each feature from the two newest releases with something new to see (2026-10-28 and 2026-10-29: Build new, the Path analyzer's new parts and Test a list, readiness, the sample tenant and snapshots, the POV planner, the ZIA policy framework export, live motion, Liquid Glass and the quality score), in the sidebar, on page titles, tabs and controls, and in Simple mode. The `*` is a button of its own: point at it, click or tap it, or Tab to it and press Enter, and it says "New in the <date> update" and what it is; screen readers hear "New in this version: <item>". A legend under the page title (the sidebar foot on the home page) says what the mark means, and Advanced settings has *Hide new marks* (this browser only). One list (`static/whats_new.js`) drives it, so when the next release with new features is added the oldest set drops off. This release adds nothing to mark, so the marks themselves are not marked.
- **Help opens only from a mark.** Help used to open whenever the pointer rested on, or focus landed on, any control. Now it opens only from a small raised `?` after a page title, a sidebar item or a control whose name does not say it all: point at it, click or tap it, or Tab to it and press Enter or Space; Esc or a click elsewhere closes it, one at a time. Each `?` is a 24px button named "Help: <item>" with aria-expanded; a sidebar item's words still open its page. Items whose name says it all (Close, Cancel, OK, Back, Download as CSV), tabs, table rows, steps in a diagram and options that describe themselves get none; the reasons are listed in `static/hover_help.js`, and the coverage test fails when a control has no entry, or no mark and none of those reasons. The words are unchanged; the browser extension works the same way.

### ZIA admin ranking decides which rule applies
- **Admin ranking is no longer ignored.** With Admin Ranking on (ZIA advanced settings, `enableAdminRankAccess`), the Path analyzer, its what-if, Test a list, workload traffic into ZIA and the shadowed-rule analysis evaluate URL filtering, Cloud App Control, SSL inspection, File Type Control, Web DLP, Cloud Firewall, DNS Control and Sandbox rules by admin rank first (0 highest), then rule order; the default rule stays last and a rank tie falls back to order. When rank, not order, picked the rule, the step says so (`decided_by: "rank"`) and shows "rank R, order N". The setting is read only when ZIA platform is ticked; not read or not readable (or missing from an older kept read or snapshot), it is never guessed: rule order is kept and a module where rank would decide differently is marked conditional with a plain caution naming both rules. The sample tenant carries the setting (off).

### Liquid Glass you can see
- **The glass now shows, in both themes, on every page, dialog and the extension.** A living backdrop sits behind the content: five soft colour fields in the brand blues, teal and violet on two fixed layers that drift very slowly (stepped transforms on the compositor, no script; still under reduced motion, paused while the tab is hidden). Cards, the sidebar, top bars, tabs, banners and dialogs are real glass: blur and saturation, a specular rim (light at the top, faint dark at the bottom), a soft inner glow, larger corners and a diagonal sheen; buttons and tabs are glass pills with a pressed state, a primary button tinted glass, and the pointer lens is brighter. A card is blurred only while it fits the window (a taller one would cost a page-sized GPU surface). Tables keep a near-opaque surface; every text colour keeps WCAG 2.2 AA against the worst point behind each surface, the new colour fields included; reduced transparency, more contrast, forced colours, print, browsers without backdrop-filter and the Glass effect switch still give solid, still surfaces. The extension's popup, side panel and settings get the same look without blur or motion, and its colours are now the app's accessible values (white on its dark primary button was 3.6:1). 32 new tests in tests/test_glass.py and tests/test_glass_browser.py.

## 2026-10-29

### Wave 6: regression and secure-by-design testing, live motion, help on hover, Liquid Glass, a quality score
- **What you asked for.** Regression testing and comprehensive secure-by-design and functionality testing, every problem fixed; live motion for every path, flow and traffic analysis; a hover pop-up on every control saying what it does and what input it expects; the Liquid Glass look; the score documented in Advanced settings; and an orchestrated workflow of checks and balances.
- **How.** 11 local agents and 22 cloud agents, each owning its own files, looped until a full pass found nothing new; two more agents closed what they left open. Then an orchestrated review: four reviewers (security, correctness, user experience and accessibility, maintainability) read the merged change, two skeptics tried to disprove each finding, a three-judge panel (security, daily admin, engineering) weighed every place two branches disagreed, and an arbiter ranked what was left. Two agents fixed what survived.
- **Findings.** 552 fixed (13 high, 157 medium, 334 low, 48 info), 22 accepted with a reason, 1 open (info: the sample tenant cannot carry every Build new catalogue without changing other pages' answers). Every fix has a test that fails without it, mapped to OWASP ASVS, CWE, WSTG, WCAG 2.2, SLSA, Scorecard or SSDF.
- **Highlights.**
  - Traffic analysis gave wrong answers in 22 places (plain HTTP shown as encrypted, IPv6-only firewall rules matching everything, HTTP-only rules said to block HTTPS, Web DLP exclusions ignored, IDN hosts refused); fixed, with oracle and property tests, and Test a list now gives each row exactly the single-destination answer.
  - Writes: Undo could be replayed or aimed at objects the app never made; two applies at once both wrote; a timed-out create was sent again blind; rule migration had no plan hash; a remediation recipe wiped whole firewall rules; one-click fixes and queued fixes skipped the Scope page. All closed, with one write run per tenant at a time.
  - 11 assessment checks passed when ZIA refused the read; they now say "not permitted".
  - Secrets: an access token echoed by a gateway reached job logs and exports; snapshots kept device and policy tokens; all masked.
  - A deadlock in the change-queue and proposals stores could freeze the app; locks are now re-entrant.
- **Live motion.** The Path analyzer (ZPA, PAC, every ZIA module, what-if, workloads), Test a list, the Flow animator, the Data Flow Map, SSL flow, SSL coverage, ZPA analysis, Traffic insights, the Live DLP feed and the Bypass report each show their result as a live flow, from the result the page already has; still under reduced motion, paused when hidden, said in words.
- **Help on hover.** Every control on every page and in the browser extension has a pop-up: what it does, what input it expects, and whether it reads or changes the tenant. It shows on hover, keyboard focus and touch; Esc closes it. CI fails when a control has none.
- **Liquid Glass.** Translucent, blurred surfaces with a light edge on the bars, sidebar, dialogs, tabs, cards and buttons; tables stay near-solid. Text keeps WCAG AA contrast in both themes; it turns solid for reduced transparency, more contrast, forced colours and print, and has a switch in Advanced settings.
- **Quality and security score** (Advanced settings, `docs/QUALITY_SCORE.md`): built from every QA record, the standards each test covers and the last full test run; open critical or high findings, or any failing test, cap it. CI fails when it is stale.
- **Verified.** Full suite 21,327 passed, 0 failed, 133 skipped (recorded in `docs/qa/test_run.json`); quality and security score 100 / 100, grade A.

## 2026-10-28

### Build, prove and predict: tenant builders, a deeper Path analyzer, readiness, sample tenant, POV planner and a no-install edition
- **What you asked for.** Every capability two other public Zscaler tools have (one builds proof-of-value tenants, one predicts traffic decisions) that this app lacked, with the overlaps compressed into this app's own pages and UI.
- **One engine for every builder.** Bulk changes (`/report/operations`) gained *Build new*: each builder is a plugin on the same engine (`admin_ops.register`), so it inherits the scope check before any read, the four write gates, the preview with a plan hash, the re-read before each write, read-back, Undo and the audit log. Nothing is created enabled unless you say so.
  - **ZIA security baseline** (Strict, Balanced, Permissive or Healthcare zero trust, with HIPAA, HITRUST, NIST, PCI DSS, CJIS, SOX, FERPA or GDPR citations) across URL filtering, Cloud Firewall, SSL inspection, Sandbox, DNS Control, File Type Control, Cloud App Control and Advanced Threat Protection; and the **SSL certificate manager** that moves Inspect rules to another certificate.
  - **ZPA builder**: App Connector group with a provisioning key shown once and never kept, segment groups, server groups, application segments (with browser access and PRA), and access, timeout, inspection and isolation policies, all under one prefix so a re-run updates in place and Undo removes them.
  - **Client Connector profiles** (forwarding and app profile from best practice or the portal default) and an install command builder on the fleet page.
  - **ZDX probe setup** (Microsoft 365 and other predefined apps, custom apps, Web and CloudPath probes).
  - **AI Guard**: route GenAI apps through AI Guard by proxy chaining, and turn a compliance profile into an AI Guard detection policy (Monitor by default).
- **Path analyzer, one answer for everything on the path.** ZPA is evaluated first (segment, bypass, client forwarding, access policy); then every ZIA module (Advanced Threat Protection, Cloud Firewall, DNS Control, Cloud App Control, URL filtering, SSL inspection, Sandbox, File Type Control, DLP) with its deciding rule, for web traffic or any protocol and port; the PAC file is evaluated without ever being run; an optional user, group, department and location what-if; traffic from a workload through Cloud & Branch Connector; and *Test a list* runs up to 250,000 destinations against what each was expected to do, with Fix selected handing off to Bulk changes.
- **Readiness after sign-in** (in Required API access and on the Connect page): what the API client's role and licences allow per product, every scope or change that will not work greyed out with the steps to ask for. It reads only the role and the licences for what is in scope.
- **Sample tenant and snapshots** (Connect page): a made-up company, Larkspur Freight, on which every page works with nothing real; save a read-only snapshot of a tenant beside its profile, open it offline, compare it with the live tenant.
- **POV planner** (`/report/pov`): intake, success criteria, runbook, *Prove it* through the Path analyzer, Insights counts, and a Word or PDF report, kept with the tenant profile.
- **ZIA policy framework export**: a new part of Export from tenant that captures URL, firewall, SSL, Sandbox, DNS and File Type Control rules to load into another tenant through the baseline builder.
- **No-install edition**: a release workflow builds a single-folder app per platform, smoke-tests it, checksums it and signs SLSA build provenance; signing with your own certificates is optional.
- **Fixes.**
  - A saved tenant profile chosen without a cloud no longer falls back to the "production" cloud: every route now uses the profile's own cloud.
  - The Data Flow Map no longer counts a path as "reaching ZIA encrypted" when SSL inspection was simply not in scope, and its text no longer claims more than it checks (it traces the configuration, not live transactions).
  - Client Connector check 6.3 no longer fails when a device's state is a number; fleet health no longer counts removed devices as registered.
  - The Connect page no longer scrolls sideways on a phone.
  - The ZIA baseline and the SSL certificate move always ask for the typed confirmation when they create or enable rules or change Advanced Threat Protection (it takes effect at once).
  - msgpack 1.2.3 (CVE-2026-114613); the SBOM and notices are regenerated.

## 2026-10-26

### Security and regression review: 15 checks, every finding fixed, mapped to standards
- **What you asked for.** Fifteen regression and vulnerability checks, run by as many agents as possible at once, each looping until nothing new is found, and every test following industry best practice and secure-by-design standards.
- **How.** Fifteen agents in parallel, one per check, each on its own branch: run the check, reproduce each finding, fix the cause with a test that fails before the fix, re-run, until a full pass finds nothing new. Every new test opens with the controls it checks (OWASP ASVS 4.0.3 Level 2, OWASP Top 10 2021, CWE, WSTG, WCAG 2.2 AA, SLSA, OpenSSF Scorecard, NIST SSDF); `docs/SECURITY_TESTING.md` lists them by standard and CI fails when a QA test names none or the document is stale. Fixes follow CISA Secure by Design: secure defaults, deny by default, fail closed, least privilege.
- **Highest-impact fixes.**
  - **Writes:** every tenant write needs all four gates (write mode, a writer, the assessor named, the typed confirmation), checked before signing in; the migration wizard, fix recipes and pack import had gaps.
  - **Login and session:** pages on other local ports could use the API; with the UI token off, look-alike loopback names passed; the SSO sign-in was not bound to the browser; unverified e-mails could make writers.
  - **Secrets:** the AI-assistant tool argument could send the client secret to any host; encoded secrets in sign-in errors, webhook URLs in digests, and Client Connector passcodes are now masked; a forgotten secret no longer survives in the backup copy.
  - **Outbound requests:** the fetcher no longer follows redirects to internal addresses or accepts parser tricks; the webhook no longer follows redirects; the OneAPI sign-in has a timeout.
  - **Injection:** tenant names can no longer inject Terraform (comments, `${}`, `%{}`), HTML (operations preview, rule migrator) or Markdown; spreadsheet formulas from lists are neutralised.
  - **Denial of service:** linear-time patterns on all user and tenant text, nesting caps, zip and tar bomb limits, large-tenant analyses that compare only the pairs that matter, uploads and reports off the event loop, bounded logs, jobs and stores.
  - **Scope:** one tenant can no longer receive another tenant's cached read or console index; Browser security reads Cloud App Control only when it is in scope.
  - **API:** no route answers 500 to malformed input; 422 errors never echo the body; error text is bounded.
  - **Files:** everything the app writes is owner-only; uploads refuse DTDs in any encoding; no route reads a file named by a traversal id.
  - **Browser extension:** it may stop only an export it started; messages are checked by sender; page text cannot break the badges.
  - **Supply chain and CI:** CI tools are hash-locked; workflow tokens are least-privilege; checkout no longer leaves the token behind; local data is kept out of the container image; the update check no longer vouches for a fork.
  - **Accessibility:** every text and button colour meets WCAG AA contrast in both themes; reduced motion stops scripted scrolling; no page scrolls sideways at 320 or 390 px, the results page with findings included.
  - **Tests themselves:** the suite is hermetic and order-independent (no files left in the repo, app settings in the shell ignored, pacing on an injected clock, browsers on ports the OS picks); a Beacon status race that made tests flaky is fixed in the app.
- **Needs the owner (GitHub settings).** Protect `main` (pull request required, the CI checks required, no force pushes); confirm Actions workflow permissions are read-only and Dependabot alerts are on.
- **Windows launchers renamed** to `*_Zscaler_Made_Easy.*` and the shortcut to *Zscaler Made Easy*; shortcuts made before the rename keep working through three forwarding files, and the next update or install renames them. A test keeps the old product name out of every file except those and the few strings needed to clean it up.
- **Verified.** Full suite 7,804 passed; every page (34) and sidebar button (48) in Chromium with no errors, external requests or writes, and no sideways scroll at 390 px.

### A companion browser extension (Chrome and Edge)
- **What you asked for.** An option to use Zscaler Made Easy as a browser extension.
- **What it is.** A small extension that works with the app on your computer rather than replacing it: the app's Python side (Zscaler's SDK, jobs that wait out ZIA's rate limit, the OS keychain, the audit log) cannot run inside a browser extension, so the work and every safeguard stay in the app.
- **What it does.** Its toolbar button shows the site you are on and *Analyze this site* opens the Path analyzer with it filled in; a right-click does the same for a link, a selected hostname, URL or IP address, or the page. The popup also opens Zscaler Made Easy, the Path analyzer, the Live DLP feed, the Flow animator and the help page, and says whether the app is running. Nothing runs until you click Analyze in the app.
- **Get it.** *Advanced settings → Browser extension → Download the extension*, unzip, and *Load unpacked* in `chrome://extensions` or `edge://extensions` (steps on the page and in `docs/BROWSER_EXTENSION.md`). Its *Settings* take the app's address (`http://127.0.0.1:8001` for the container).
- **Security.** No credentials or tenant data in the extension; permissions `activeTab`, `contextMenus` and `storage` only (no access to the pages you browse); it talks only to 127.0.0.1 or localhost and refuses any other address; an address loses its query string and fragment before it is passed on; a selection is used only when it is one hostname, URL or IP address; its one request is the app's `GET /api/ping`, and an answer other than Zscaler Made Easy's is reported as such.
- **Verified.** New `tests/test_browser_extension.py` (6 tests: permissions and CSP, no remote code or credentials, loopback-only addresses and trimmed destinations under node, the zip the app serves, the docs). The extension was loaded unpacked in Chromium against the running app: the popup found the app and opened its pages, the Path analyzer was pre-filled, the settings refused an outside address, and the right-click menus registered.

### Export from tenant: dictionaries, engines and rules separately, as JSON or Terraform
- **What you asked for.** Download dictionaries, engines and rules separately, and not only as JSON: Terraform as well, as a choice.
- **What to export.** Tick *Dictionaries*, *Engines* and/or *Rules*, and pick the ones you want in each list: every custom dictionary (clones included) and every custom engine is listed, including engines and dictionaries no rule uses, which could not be exported before.
- **Include what they use** (on by default, the old behaviour): engines bring the dictionaries they use, rules the engines and dictionaries they use. Off: only what you picked; what it uses is named, not copied, and listed as *named, not included* (the result, pack.json `referenced_only`, and a comment in the Terraform), because it must already exist under that name where it goes.
- **Format.** *JSON pack* (the same zip as before, which the library accepts), *Terraform* (a workspace for the official zscaler/zia provider, from the same generator as the Terraform hand-off: every rule DISABLED, the assessor named in every description or asked for by `terraform apply`, what is not included looked up by name with data sources) or *both* in one zip.
- **One part at a time.** After the export, *Download one part* offers each part's own file: `<pack>-dictionaries.json`, `<pack>-engines.json`, `<pack>-rules.json`, and `<pack>-dictionaries.tf`, `<pack>-engines.tf`, `<pack>-rules.tf` for Terraform, beside the whole zip. A single .tf file is for review or to copy into an existing workspace; applying needs the rest of the workspace, which is in the zip.
- **Unchanged.** Read-only (no write mode), only what is in scope is read (an export of dictionaries and engines alone reads no rule), an empty pick is refused before any read, Stop gives no file. The default (rules with what they use, as a JSON pack) produces exactly the zip it did.
- **Verified.** New `tests/test_pack_export_parts.py` (15 tests): each part alone and with or without dependencies, picks by id and what cannot be picked, a dictionaries-only pack deployed into an empty tenant, Terraform per part (fmt-clean, by-name lookups and the note, the assessor), the zip layouts and each part's file, the job's per-part downloads (no other member, no path tricks), refusals before any read, and the picker's engine list. The flow was driven end to end in Chromium against a fake tenant.

## 2026-10-04

### Security review: exposure, request limits, secrets and logging
- **What you asked for.** A security and quality review against CISA Secure by Design, OWASP ASVS and API, FastAPI, supply-chain and CI/CD practice, with the high- and medium-severity fixes that are safe and backward-compatible.
- **Network exposure.** The container no longer starts with `ZSCALER_UI_AUTH=0`: it listens on 0.0.0.0 inside, so any process that could reach it (another container on its network) had the API with no credential. A server started some other way (`uvicorn --host 0.0.0.0`) is caught per request: with the gate off only loopback connections with a loopback `Host` name are served (also stops DNS rebinding). The rate limiter now runs ahead of the token check, so unauthenticated floods cannot fill and rotate away the audit log.
- **Request size.** The body cap is now the outermost layer and counts chunked bodies as they stream in; before, it only read `Content-Length` and ran after the audit log had already buffered the whole body. Refusals are logged as `request_too_large`, without the body.
- **Secrets.** Ticking *remember* without a usable OS credential store (Linux, the container, or a store that refuses it) no longer writes the secret to the profile file in plain text: it is not remembered and the page says so. `DLP_CHECK_SECRET_STORE=file` is the explicit opt-in (for the container, put it in `.env`). Windows and macOS keep using the pinned `keyring`. Secrets saved by older versions still load. `local_settings.env` (install.sh) and `server.log` (start.sh) are created owner-only.
- **Logging.** The audit log keeps uploaded content (log bundles, PAC files, vendor exports, DLP test corpora) out entirely, recording only its size; secret-looking keys and the classic sign-in identity stay redacted and operational fields (pack ids, channels) are kept.
- **Remediation.** Path parameters of a remediation recipe (for example the rule id) are validated and encoded, so a value cannot retarget the write to another API object.
- **Supply chain.** On top of dev's pinned Actions, Trivy and hash-locked test tools: the Python base image is pinned by digest, the image build no longer runs an unhashed `pip install --upgrade pip`, installers and the updater refuse an unhashed install when `requirements.lock` is missing, `.dockerignore` keeps local customer data out of the image, and the CI container smoke test runs with the UI token gate on (a fixed CI token, and a request without it must get 401).
- **Unchanged.** Localhost by default, read-only by default with the typed write confirmation, disabled and monitor-only policy packs, and every API contract.
- **Verified.** New `tests/test_security_hardening_review.py` (18 tests) plus updated tests; full suite on the current dev. No tenant contacted.

Then each channel that raised an incident carries bits through dictionaries, the engine, its channel rule and the evidence lane to Workflow Automation, faster the more it raised. Each channel rule is tagged ENFORCE when every incident on it was enforced, MONITOR when any was only logged; channels with none stay idle. The page tests now check the chain on the Live DLP feed and its absence from the Path analyzer. The feed was driven in a headless browser with three incidents: no script errors.

### An upstream warning must be confirmed by two Beacon runs
- **What you saw.** Any drift detected once could immediately raise the global *Upstream change* banner, including a transient or incomplete upstream response.
- **Now.** A first detection is recorded in Beacon details as pending confirmation. The banner appears only when the exact watcher and finding persist in the next comparable run (online-to-online or offline-to-offline), and disappears immediately when a later run clears it.
- **Verified.** Tests cover the first-run hold, second-run confirmation, immediate clear, and the status endpoint exposing only confirmed drift to the banner.

### Upstream check: a preview SDK that does not cover an API yet is a note, not drift
- **What you asked for.** "Fix the upstream drift."
- **What it reported.** Two drift items, both from `zscaler-sdk-python` 2.0.0b2 (a beta): no create operation for `/endPointDlpRules` or `/casbDlpRules` in its ZIA service definition.
- **Why that was not drift.** The beta's definition does not describe those two resources at all (no list, read, update or delete either), so it is an incomplete preview, not a changed API. The stable SDK the app runs (1.9.44) and the newest release (1.11.0) both still have them, and 1.11.0 changes none of the 25 modules the app calls.
- **Now** a resource a prerelease does not describe at all is a note that names the gap and says to re-check before moving to 2.x. It is still drift in a stable release, when a described resource has lost its create operation, and when a field the app sends is not listed. The live check now reports no drift.
- **Verified.** Run against the live upstreams (PyPI, the 2.0.0b2 package): OK, no drift. New tests for the prerelease note, the stable-release drift and a described resource without its create operation. Full suite green.

## 2026-10-02

### The sidebar can be rearranged
- **What you saw.** The sidebar's items and sections (Workflow, Start, Find the gaps, Close the gaps, Reference, Troubleshoot) were in a fixed order.
- **Now.** Drag an item to another place in its section or into another section, or drag a section heading to move the whole section; a line shows where it will land, and a grip appears on hover. A click still opens the item; only a real drag moves it. From the keyboard, focus an item and press Alt+↑/↓ to move it one place through the sidebar, or Alt+Shift+↑/↓ to move its section; each move is announced and the focus stays on the item. The order is kept in this browser by each item's id, so items the app adds later appear in their usual place and removed ones are forgotten; a damaged saved order is ignored. *Sidebar order → Reset sidebar order* at the bottom of the sidebar puts the default back. On narrow screens dragging is off and the keys still work. The ① ② ③ numbers on the step strip are workflow steps and do not change.

### ZIA's hourly limit stops the assessment's reads and writes, and one 429 holds every job on the tenant
- **What you saw.** When ZIA refused a request for its hourly limit (429, *Retry-After: 3300 seconds*), the assessment client used by assessments, Beacon, path analysis, the live feed, remediation and the rule migrator waited only 30 seconds and tried each request four times, and every later check did the same, so a run sent hundreds of refused requests. A 429 seen by an assessment did not hold an export or a deploy's plan reads on the same tenant, and the export's hold after a refused read held no reads at all.
- **Now.** A 429 asking for more than 30 seconds, or naming an hourly limit, is not retried: the tenant is held for the whole wait and the request answers *rate limited* at once, as the classic API and the export already did. While that hold lasts, every job on the tenant (any client, any process) sends nothing and its checks are reported unanswered. A refused read, by the assessment client or the export, holds every job's reads on the tenant until ZIA's wait has passed; a hold after a refused write still leaves reads alone.

### The credential store for remembered secrets is part of the pinned install
- **What you saw.** A remembered profile secret goes to Windows Credential Manager or the macOS Keychain only through the `keyring` package, which the app did not install. SECURITY.md said to run an unpinned `pip install keyring`, which installed six packages that were not hash-verified and were missing from the SBOM and the notices.
- **Now.** `keyring` and the packages it needs are pinned by hash in `requirements.lock` for Windows and macOS, and they are listed in `docs/sbom.cdx.json` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` with their licences. The installers install them with the rest of the app. On Linux and in the container there is no credential store, so a remembered secret stays in the owner-only profile file, as before. The tests never touch this machine's real credential store.

### Classic-API sign-ins: the admin login stays out of the browser's records
- **What you saw.** After *Authenticate* with the classic ZIA API sign-in, the guide kept the admin login in the browser's storage across restarts, and a failure the server answered without a tenant label (a rate limit, for example) was titled *Could not connect to <admin login>*. The sidebar's *Tenant* line showed a vanity domain left in the hidden OneAPI field.
- **Now.** The guide matches the tenant on a one-way key of cloud and login, names it *classic API (<cloud>)*, and drops an old record that held the login. The sidebar shows *classic API · <cloud>*.

### CI runs only pinned tooling
- **What you saw.** CI installed `pytest` and `httpx` unpinned on top of the hash-verified lock (and `httpx2` with failures hidden), upgraded pip to whatever was newest, ran `aquasec/trivy:latest` and referenced every GitHub Action by a movable tag, so a new release or a moved tag changed what CI ran without anyone noticing.
- **Now.** The test tools come from the new hash-pinned `requirements-dev.lock` (compiled against `requirements.lock`; `make lock-dev`, and `make lock` runs it too), which CI checks for staleness like the app's lock. pip is pinned by version, Trivy by version and image digest, and every action by full commit SHA (Dependabot keeps them current). pyproject's `dev` extra carries the same pins.

### Smaller fixes
- The Terraform hand-off offers gap mode for a complete classic-API sign-in (it only looked at the OneAPI client ID).
- Best practices opened in its own tab stops its 30-second refresh when the tab is closed.
- *FP/TP detection ratio* with pop-ups blocked says so and reads nothing (it used to read the tenant and show nothing).
- *How a deployment works* follows the light theme.

### More tools read only what is in scope
- **What you saw.** Deploy Studio's rule picker and *Export from tenant* read the DLP rules of every channel (and the SaaS application tenants) whatever the Scope page said. DLP efficacy (score, export, corpus test) read the Web DLP rules with Inline Web DLP covered elsewhere, and it, the ZCC endpoint diagnostics and every step of the Evaluate All Rules migration read the tenant with nothing ticked.
- **Now.** They take the Scope page's selection, else the chosen profile's saved one. The rule picker and the export read only the DLP channels marked Zscaler; DLP efficacy needs DLP ticked and reads the Web DLP rules only with Inline Web DLP on Zscaler (the *unused by rules* findings then say *not in scope*); the ZCC diagnostics need ZCC ticked; the migration needs DLP ticked and Inline Web DLP on Zscaler. With nothing in scope, each refuses before signing in.

### Proposals are applied only to their own tenant, also with the classic API
- **What you saw.** *Preview*, *Apply* and *Roll back* on an approved FP/TP proposal used whatever credentials the assessment page held: after switching tenant, a proposal could be written to a same-named rule of another tenant. With the classic ZIA API sign-in, all three said *Tenant credentials are needed*.
- **Now.** They refuse (409) before signing in when the credentials are for another tenant than the proposal's, and accept either sign-in.

### The assessor is never the tool
- **What you saw.** An assessor named *Zscaler Made Easy* (or *dlp check*, *Zscaler Made Easy pack*) was accepted by deploy, the single-pack import, the Terraform hand-off and the library download, so pushed descriptions said *[DLP-Check pack ...]*; only the Terraform CLI refused it.
- **Now.** One rule (`(?i)dlp[\W_]*check|zscaler[\W_]*made[\W_]*easy`) refuses the tool's name in any spelling everywhere: the API (400, before signing in, also from a saved profile), the CLI, `var.assessor` in every workspace, Deploy Studio and Advanced settings.

### Classic-API tenants: Stop and ZIA's rate limits hold for every request
- **What you saw.** With the classic ZIA API sign-in, the SDK's helper answered a 429 itself: it slept ZIA's whole Retry-After (close to an hour on the hourly limit) with no cap, ignored Stop during that wait, then sent the request again — a write could reach the tenant after Stop. Its requests also bypassed the app's pacing, so classic-API reads and writes were not held to ZIA's read and write limits.
- **Now.** Every classic-API request goes through the app's paced session (the same read and write buckets as OneAPI) and is checked against Stop before it is sent and during any wait. A short 429 is waited out at most twice, up to 30 seconds each, in steps Stop ends; a longer Retry-After is not waited for inside the request but answered like OneAPI's (*Retry wait time … exceeds configured maxRetrySeconds 30*), so a deployment or export waits it out in its own stoppable steps or stops with the reason.

### A classic-API user can run the assessment
- **What you saw.** With the classic ZIA API sign-in filled in, *Run assessment* did nothing but ask for the vanity domain, client ID and client secret: fields the classic sign-in hides. The guide's *Connect* step focused the hidden vanity domain.
- **Now.** The run asks for the chosen sign-in's own fields (admin username, password and API key for the classic API), and no vanity domain is sent.

### Stop pressed before a run has started still stops it
- **What you saw.** Stop pressed while a deployment, removal, export, Terraform hand-off or Path analysis was still signing in did nothing, and the run then went ahead in full. Stop on the Live DLP feed while its first poll was answering left one more tenant read 15 seconds later, and Stop then Start could leave two polling loops running at twice the pace.
- **Now.** Each start carries a random start token; a Stop sent before the job's id arrives (`POST /api/dlp-packs/jobs/stop-before-start`) makes the job start stopped, so nothing is read or written, and the job is also cancelled by id once it exists. The live feed runs one polling loop at a time and makes no read after Stop.

### The SSL inspection flow and the Bypass report read only what is in scope
- **What you saw.** Both read every section of the tenant (SSL inspection, Web DLP, URL filtering, firewall rules and Client Connector) whatever the Scope page said, and the SSL flow's auto-refresh repeated that every two minutes.
- **Now.** They take the Scope page's selection, else the chosen profile's saved one, and read only the parts in scope; the rest say *not in scope*. The SSL flow needs SSL inspection on Zscaler; the firewall rules come with the ZIA platform scope. With nothing in scope, each refuses before signing in.

### The Windows install can start the MCP server; PyJWT 2.15.0
- **What you saw.** On Windows, `python dlp_mcp_server.py` failed with *No module named 'pywintypes'*: `requirements.lock` was compiled on Linux and left out the Windows-only `pywin32` (needed by `mcp`) and `colorama` (needed by `typer`), and the installer's `--no-deps` install never added them. CI's `pip-audit --strict` also failed on the lock's PyJWT 2.14.0 (CVE-2026-101918, a RecursionError on unverified decodes; the app's signature-verified SSO decode does not reach it).
- **Now.** The lock is resolved for every platform at once (`make lock` runs `uv pip compile --universal`), so `pywin32` and `colorama` are pinned by hash behind `sys_platform == 'win32'` markers and Linux, macOS and the container skip them. PyJWT is at 2.15.0 (floor raised in `requirements.txt` and `pyproject.toml`); no other pin moved. CI's lock check uses the same universal compile, a new `windows-lock` job installs the lock on Windows with `--no-deps` and runs `pip check` and the MCP server import, and the CycloneDX SBOM is committed as `docs/sbom.cdx.json` (`docs/SBOM.md` says how to regenerate it).

### Wording
- The Beacon button says the optional tenant watch reads only the endpoints in scope on the Scope page, GET only, about one request a second.
- Advanced settings says the assessor's name is required for a Terraform download, never replaced by Zscaler Made Easy.

### A classic-API tenant is never named by its admin login or a stale vanity domain
- **What you saw.** With the classic ZIA API sign-in, Beacon's tenant watch, the Path analyzer, the live feed and the sign-in check named the tenant `admin@acme.com (cloud, classic API)`, and Beacon kept that label on disk. The Data Flow Map, Bypass and SSL flow reports named it by a vanity domain left in the hidden OneAPI field.
- **Now.** Every label, kept report, export and Beacon snapshot names a classic-API tenant `classic API (cloud)`. Beacon history written before is cleaned of the login when it is read, and the same tenant still finds its baseline. An authentication error masks the login. The assessment page sends only the chosen sign-in's fields.

### The Data Flow Map, the FP/TP Detection Ratio and the live feed read only what is in scope
- **What you saw.** *Traffic flow & gaps* read Client Connector and every ZIA section whatever the Scope page said. The manual FP/TP Detection Ratio and the live feed read every channel's rules and Workflow Automation's incidents.
- **Now.** They take the Scope page's selection, else the chosen profile's saved one (never mixed). The Data Flow Map reads only the parts in scope and draws the rest as *not in scope*. The FP/TP Detection Ratio reads the rules of the DLP channels marked Zscaler, and the incidents only when Workflow Automation is marked Zscaler. The live feed needs DLP ticked and Workflow Automation on Zscaler. With nothing in scope, each one refuses before signing in.

### Stop stops the Beacon tenant watch and the plan read of a hand-off, a deployment and a removal
- **What you saw.** A running tenant watch could not be stopped. After Stop, a Terraform gap hand-off, a deployment and a removal still finished their tenant read (a removal also read the tenant again to verify, with nothing deleted).
- **Now.** The Beacon tab has a Stop button (`POST /api/beacon/stop`). No further endpoint is read, and the watch is kept as one that stopped early. Stop during a plan read sends no further read. A deployment or removal stopped before anything was written does not read the tenant back.

### Terraform workspaces generated without an assessor need one at apply time
- **What you saw.** The workspace written when a pack is uploaded or rebuilt, and the ones committed under `terraform/<pack>/`, tagged every description `[DLP-Check pack …]`.
- **Now.** Without an assessor's name, the tag reads `[${var.assessor} pack …]`, and `variable "assessor"` is required (no default; no brackets, not the tool's name). `terraform apply` cannot push anything until an assessor is named. Downloads that name the assessor are unchanged.
- **Also.** Each workspace README's *Use* section shows `terraform plan -var 'assessor=…'` and says why it is required, `POLICY_PACKS.md` says the same for the committed workspaces, and `python pack_terraform.py … --assessor "Jane Doe"` writes the name in (a name naming the tool is refused).

### Beacon's tenant watch reads no channel the assessment leaves out
- **What you saw.** With only Endpoint (or Email) DLP marked Zscaler, the tenant watch for DLP still read the Inline Web DLP rules, the SSL inspection rules, the Cloud App Control rules and the tenancy restrictions; with GenAI & prompts ticked and GenAI Security left unticked it read 9 endpoints, while the assessment for the same selection reads none of them. The watch kept its own, older channel rule: outside DLP only an *alternative* channel took checks out, and only three rule endpoints needed their channel.
- **Now.** The watch uses the assessment's own rule (`channel_scope.partition` and its endpoint map) for every scope: a check's endpoints are read only when the assessment would run that check, and a channel's own configuration only with that channel on Zscaler. A channel the Beacon tab does not send counts as unticked. As a backstop, the watch's GETs go through the same client guard as the assessment's, so a channel's endpoint is refused without touching the tenant even if a selection listed it. With GenAI & prompts ticked and GenAI Security unticked there is nothing to read, and the run is refused before signing in.

### The assessor's name is mandatory for every pack push; nothing is tagged Zscaler Made Easy
- **What you saw.** With the assessor field blank, a pack deploy still tagged its dictionaries, engines and rules `[DLP-Check pack …]`.
- **Now.** Deploy policy packs, the single-pack import, the Terraform hand-off (gap and static) and the library's Terraform download all refuse without an assessor name: *Enter the assessor's name (Scope assessment → Assessor name) before pushing a policy pack.* The request stops before the tenant is signed in to, and the page checks first, so nothing is read or written. The saved profile's assessor counts. Planning a single-pack import still works without one, since it writes nothing.
- **Unchanged.** Objects tagged `[DLP-Check …]` by earlier deploys are still recognised by removal, export and the journey check.

### Export from tenant: rate refusals, empty picks and Stop
- **What you saw.** When ZIA refused a read, the export held every request to the tenant until `zia_rate.full_reset('read')`, the hour's read window, even for a short per-second 429; an hourly refusal (the SDK's `RetryTooLong`, *Retry wait time 3600 seconds…*) was waited out and read again. An empty or blank pick still signed in to the tenant before finding nothing to export, and Stop pressed while the pack was being zipped still handed out a pack.
- **Now.** An hourly refusal (a `RetryTooLong` asking to wait 300 s or more) fails the export straight away, with no further read and no pack missing a channel. A short 429 is waited out for 5 s, then 15 s, a `RetryTooLong` under 300 s for the time ZIA asks for (plus 1 s), and Resource Access Blocked for 30 s then 60 s, and the read is sent again; it never waits for the hour's window. Each wait can be stopped. Ticking no rule, or sending a blank pick, answers HTTP 400 *No rule was picked: tick at least one rule (or pick "all" for a channel) to export.* before the tenant is signed in to, so nothing is read. Stop pressed while the pack is built ends the export as *stopped*, with no pack.

## 2026-10-01

### Help-center pages are read again: Beacon and the Updates tab see the real articles
- **What you saw.** On Beacon (and the *Zscaler SDK & docs updates* tab) all seven watched help-center pages said *no readable content (119 characters of text)*. help.zscaler.com now renders in the browser: every URL answers the same empty shell, so the pages could never show a change.
- **Now.** A help.zscaler.com page is read the way the site reads it, through its article answer (`/zapi/fetch-data`); its title and body are what is checked and hashed (for a documentation index such as *Zscaler Deployments & Operations*, its list of guides, so a guide added or removed shows as a change). Other sites are read as before. When the article answer cannot be used (a brief 5xx, a proxy's block page, a malformed answer) the check says so as an error and keeps the page's own link; it does not read the empty shell and call the page gone.
- **Moved pages.** Three watched articles no longer exist and point at their replacements: ZPA API (`/zpa/about-api-key-management`), First Match Wins vs Evaluate All Rules (`/zia/configuring-dlp-policy-rules-evaluate-all-rules-mode-enabled`, also the source link on the Evaluate All Rules tab and in the wizard) and GenAI (`/zia/adding-ai-ml-applications-rule-cloud-app-control`, also the Word report's fallback citation). A page that answers 404 shows *moved or gone* on Beacon, and one in maintenance (the OneAPI page today) *not readable*, with the help center's own reason.
- **One-time new baseline.** The first check after this update reads each page a new way, so it records a new baseline instead of reporting every page as changed; later checks compare against it. A watched page whose address changes starts over the same way. On Beacon, a page that was readable and now answers 404 is reported as gone, not changed.
- **No false alarm when a page comes back, no missed change after a failed read.** On Beacon, a page back from maintenance (or a 404) is compared with the last time it was readable, so the same text is not reported as changed; a run where a page could not be read, or was read as the shell once, no longer becomes the baseline that hides the next real change. A network error on the article answer is reported straight away instead of also fetching the page (which doubled the wait on a timeout); this holds for the Updates tab, Beacon and the best-practices crawler's title lookup alike, and a rate-limited (429) answer is not followed by a second request either.
- **Updates tab.** An article in maintenance shows *NOT READABLE* and keeps its own link (it is not sent to the section index as a gone page). An article the help center returns is no longer called dead because its title contains words such as *Removed*, *Error* or *Access Denied*. The Word report's generic fallback cites the live *Zscaler Deployments & Operations* page.
- **Best-practices library.** Titles of help-center articles found through the sitemap come from the article answer instead of the shell's generic title. Pages on other sites keep their own <title> as before. A site that answers 429 is asked for no more titles in that crawl, and one article whose answer cannot be read keeps its slug title instead of stopping the crawl.
- **Beacon's change list.** A page going into maintenance is listed as *not readable* (not *page gone*), and a page removed after maintenance is still reported as gone.
- **SaaS capability matrix.** Continuous evaluation's matrix refresh reads its two help-center pages through the article answer too, so it can refresh again.

### Signing in reads nothing; an assessment reads only what you put in scope
- **What you asked for.** Authenticating (the Authenticate button, unlock, choosing or saving a tenant profile) must not read the tenant, because every read counts against its ZIA API rate limit. Nothing is assessed until you run it, and then only what the Scope page puts in scope.
- **Authenticate only signs in.** `POST /api/tenant/check` obtains the token (or the classic API's session) and nothing else: it no longer lists dictionaries and engines or counts Web, Endpoint, SaaS and Email DLP rules. It answers *Signed in to <tenant>. Sign-in only: the tenant's configuration was not read*; a failure still says why and what to do.
- **Nothing starts an assessment by itself.** Enter in a field no longer runs it, and a Run click while signed out no longer queues a run that Authenticate then starts: it says *Sign in first … then click Run assessment again*.
- **Nothing in scope is refused.** `/api/assess` with no `scopes` answers 400 (*choose what to assess on the Scope page*) instead of running every check. An MCP or CLI caller that sets the legacy `scope` on purpose still runs as before, journey included when that scope covers DLP (`dlp`, `zia`, `full`, `all`).
- **An unticked channel is not read.** A channel left unticked on the Scope page (a gap) now has its checks reported as *Not in scope*, outside the score, like an *Alternative* one; the report still lists it as a gap. A check shared with a channel marked Zscaler still runs. With no channel declaration at all (MCP, CLI) every check in the selected scopes runs, as before.
- **The journey reads only what you chose.** The assessment's data-governance journey runs only when DLP is in scope, only for the policy packs ticked on the tenant profile (no packs ticked: no journey, never "every installed pack" or the industry's packs), and preflights each pack only on its channels marked Zscaler.
- **Starting the server reads no tenant.** The Digest's continuous evaluation no longer signs in to every profile that remembers its secret 30 seconds after start and every 6 hours: it runs when you click *Run the loop now* (or `continuous_eval.py --once`), or on a timer only when you opt in with `DLP_CHECK_CONTINUOUS_EVAL_HOURS` (unset or 0: off).
- **An unticked channel's rules are never read, whatever section a check sits in.** A check that reads a channel's own configuration (Web DLP rules and global options, Endpoint DLP rules, Email DLP rules, SaaS rules and tenants, SSL inspection rules, Cloud App Control rules) runs only when that channel is on Zscaler; the checks' client also refuses such a read outright. Once any channel is declared, a channel left out of the declaration (or sent as null) counts as unticked. The journey's preflight lists Web DLP rules only when web is in scope.
- **Unknown or blank scopes are refused (400)** before signing in, like an empty one, and so is a legacy `scope` that names nothing; a run whose ticked scopes all fall in unticked channels is refused with the channel to tick.
- **A run nobody listens to stops**: closing the page, the progress card's new **Stop** button or *New assessment* cancels the checks still queued, and the journey makes no further tenant read once the run has ended or its measurement timed out. Authenticate, the assessment's checks, the journey (in the assessment and on its own) and the Digest's loop end the session they open, each only once nothing is still reading on it (the classic ZIA API's admin session no longer idles out on its own).
- **A refused run says why, where you started it.** *Nothing to assess … Tick the channel* is shown on the Scope page with focus on Run assessment, and the dashboard tiles go back to what they showed; it is no longer reported as *Connection lost … partial results*. Authenticate replaces only a *Sign in first* note, never the Report details warning.
- **A channel declaration nobody can read narrows everything.** A `channels` object whose entries are all unreadable (a blank key, an unknown id sent as null or a number) counts every channel as unticked, never as *no declaration*.
- **The Digest's loop reads only each profile's saved scope** (as *The Digest's loop reads a tenant only when you run it* below describes, which this builds on). On top of it: Workflow Automation's incidents are read only when Workflow Automation is marked as run on Zscaler (with it unticked or a gap, FP/TP Detection Ratio reads the in-scope channels' rules and no incidents; covered by another product, the ratio is not read at all), and the loop ends each profile's session when it is done with it, so a classic ZIA API session is not left open. A timer value under one hour leaves the timer off.
- **A read the tenant did not answer is *Could not be checked*.** A check whose read was rate limited (HTTP 429), unavailable (502/503/504) or timed out is reported as an error, outside the score, never as *Configured* or an empty tenant (*0 devices OK*, *No PAC files*).
- **A Path Analyzer link no longer runs by itself** (`?run=1` was a tenant read on page load): the fields are filled in, you click Analyze.
- **The Scope page and docs/SCORING.md say** that a check of a ticked channel which also reads another channel's rules runs only when that channel is ticked too. The check map's drift test now feeds dict-shaped answers as well and caught CA.5 (Cloud App Control rules).

### Classic-API tenants on one cloud are told apart by a keyed fingerprint
- **What was wrong.** A classic-API tenant is named only *classic API (cloud)*, so two customers on the same cloud looked alike to the undo and removal checks and to the rate-limit history. The record's tenant key was a plain hash of the cloud and the admin login: anyone who knew a login could compute it and match it to a record or to the rate state.
- **Now.** Each deployment made with the classic API keeps a fingerprint of its sign-in: HMAC-SHA256 of the cloud and the admin login (lower-cased) under a key of this install, cut to 16 hex characters. It cannot be turned back into the login or the domain, and without the install's key it cannot be worked out from a known login. Undo and removal by deployment refuse a record whose fingerprint differs from the current credentials' (*made in another classic API (cloud) tenant … Switch tenant first*). The rate-limit forecast, its track record and the rate state count a classic-API tenant under its fingerprint, so two tenants' requests, refusals and runs never merge, and no hash of the login is written any more. The fingerprint is never shown or returned by the API; the record names no login or domain.
- **The key.** 32 random bytes in `tenant_fingerprint.key`, created on first use next to `deployments.json` (owner-only, git-ignored and kept out of the image; on `/data` in the container), or at `DLP_CHECK_FINGERPRINT_KEY`. Keep it with `deployments.json`: a new key gives every classic-API tenant a new fingerprint, and records made under the old one are then refused for undo (remove them by pack instead).
- **Records made before.** A record without a fingerprint, under *classic API (cloud)* or the older vanity-domain label, is matched by label as before. One that carries the earlier plain-hash key is still accepted for its own tenant. OneAPI tenants are unchanged: named by vanity domain and told apart by their API client as before.

### Pack deploys name only the assessor; the classic API's vanity domain and login stay out
- **What you saw.** Every object a pack deploy creates said `[DLP-Check pack <id> v1]` in its description, and the deployment record and on-screen tenant label were built from the vanity domain even when you signed in with the classic API (admin login, no vanity domain).
- **Now.** The tag names the assessor from Scope assessment: `[Jane Doe pack <id> v1]` (baselines: `[Jane Doe baseline <id> v4] Measured on every journey check…`; Terraform: `… Terraform-managed.`). With no assessor name it said DLP-Check (the app's former name); superseded on 2026-10-02: every pack push now requires the assessor's name. The name is cleaned so it cannot break the tag (no brackets or line breaks, at most 40 bytes, so the tag always fits ZIA's 255-byte description limit), and the deployment record stores the same cleaned name. Every pack path uses it: Deploy policy packs, the single-pack import, the Terraform hand-off and the pack library's Terraform download on Advanced settings (its *Assessor named in Terraform downloads* field, filled from Scope assessment when the tab was opened from it). The name is looked up once per run, so the tags and the record always name the same person, and a name starting "DLP-Check pack" or "DLP-Check baseline" is written "DLP Check …" so it cannot pass for another pack's tag.
- **Records and labels.** A deployment record keeps the assessor's name ("by Jane Doe" in the history) and nothing else about who signed in. With the classic API the tenant is shown and recorded as *classic API (cloud)*: no vanity domain, no admin login.
- **Nothing already deployed is orphaned.** Removal, export, the journey check and the pack library recognise both the new tag and the old `[DLP-Check …]` one, so packs deployed before still remove cleanly, and a deployment recorded under the old label can still be undone. A tag is recognised only at the end of a description and, for an assessor's tag, only for the pack being removed, so a customer's own bracketed text never qualifies an object for removal. The pick-to-remove inventory, *Remove every deployed policy pack* and Export from tenant count an assessor's tag at the end of a description when it names a pack in the library or carries what every deploy writes (a version, or the deploy's closing sentence), so objects of a pack since removed from the library are still found while a customer's `[Q3 pack review]` is neither shown as Zscaler Made Easy-tagged nor stripped from an export. Versions and pack ids are free text: `[Jane Doe pack acme v1.0 beta]` and a pack id with a space are read back like the old tag, so a redeploy replaces the tag instead of adding a second one. The Terraform gap hand-off, Export from tenant, the assessment's journey and the rate-limit forecast also name a classic-API tenant *classic API (cloud)*, and the forecast still counts records written under the old label. Because every classic-API tenant on a cloud shares that label, a record also keeps something that tells the tenant apart without naming it (see *Classic-API tenants on one cloud are told apart by a keyed fingerprint*), so one customer's forecast and undo never pick up another classic-API customer's runs.

### The Path analyzer shows the DLP chain live, with bits that stop where the path breaks
- **What you asked for.** A live motion visual on the Path analyzer, with moving bits that follow the flows in the Data Security Architect reference (*The chain, end to end*).
- **Now.** Above the result, *The DLP chain, live* draws every stage: content captured on a channel (inline web, endpoint, email, SaaS at rest) → dictionaries → the DLP engine (Channels gate, at most four engines per rule) → the channel rule (Web, Endpoint, Email or SaaS API DLP; DSPM beside them with its own alert flow) → the lanes out of a rule: the evidence lane (Incident Receiver or cloud-to-cloud → customer storage → storage event → the WA integration → Workflow Automation), the telemetry lane (logs → NSS → SIEM, which never feeds Workflow Automation), auditor email and third-party ICAP beside them.
- **Before a result** every lane idles with bits flowing. **After Analyze** the bits follow the path the result describes and stop at the link that breaks it, marked with a ✕ (or a ■ where policy blocks it) and said in words: traffic that bypasses Zscaler, is left encrypted, is blocked before DLP, has no covering Web DLP rule or no engine, or a rule that forwards no evidence (so Workflow Automation stays empty). Endpoint, Email and SaaS show how far the same data gets on each.
- **Motion** is inline SVG with no new library, pauses when the page is hidden or scrolled away, has a Pause / Play button, and starts paused (static arrows) when your system asks for reduced motion. A list under the picture says the same path in words for screen readers. Nothing new is read from the tenant: it draws the result the analyzer already returned.
- **Readable and steady.** A broken lane is drawn only on its own stretch, so the live telemetry and auditor links beside it on the forwarding bus keep their look. The chain's text is 11px or more at every window width (a narrow window scrolls it sideways), and the words under it name at most five rules or engines per line, then say how many more. If the picture cannot draw a result, it is hidden and the result itself still shows.
- **Upload blocks and amber.** A Cloud App Control or File Type Control rule that blocks uploads before DLP gets its ■ on the way in, and the words under the picture name it, whatever DLP does. Bits stopping at a ■ fade in the block's colour, not the red of a break. The legend explains the amber lane: an evidence path that is assumed (ICAP only), not confirmed.
- **Rules that could not be read are not "no rule".** If ZIA refuses or rate-limits a rule read (HTTP 429, 403, a timeout), the hop, the channel and the verdict say *Could not read* with the reason, the chain marks it with a ?, and nothing tells you to deploy a rule that may already exist. Run it again once the API answers.
- **A link never starts a read.** Opening `/report/path?d=<host>&run=1` used to analyze on page load. A link now only fills the form in; the tenant is read when you click Analyze (or press Enter).
- **Stop really stops the reads.** Stop used to be checked only before and after the whole ZIA read, so the analysis went on reading every ZIA section (and sat out a 429 pause, then retried) after you pressed it. Stop is now checked before every Client Connector and ZIA request, between catalogue pages and Cloud App Control rule types, before the URL lookup, and inside a request that is waiting out ZIA's rate limit or a 429 pause. Once pressed nothing more is sent, the job ends as *Stopped*, and no partial read is shown or kept for the next destination.
- **Only what is in scope is read.** The analyzer used to read every channel whatever the Scope page said. It now reads only what is in scope there: Client Connector and PAC files with ZCC ticked; URL filtering and File Type Control with DLP or ZIA platform ticked; SSL inspection, Cloud App Control, Web, Endpoint, Email and SaaS DLP rules only with their channel marked Zscaler (a gap, an alternative or an undeclared channel is not read); the URL categories and ZIA's URL lookup only when a ZIA policy section is in scope. The page sends the Scope page's ticks from the assessment tab; with none, the chosen profile's saved selection is used (never mixed with the page's); with nothing in scope the request is refused before signing in. A part out of scope says *Not in scope* on its hop, its channel, the verdict, a new *Not in scope* list and the live chain (marked –), never "no rule" and never "could not read", and no fix is suggested for it.
- **A kept read is reused only for the same scope.** The tenant read kept for the next destination is stamped with what it read; a wider or narrower scope reads the tenant again.

## 2026-09-30

### The Digest's loop reads a tenant only when you run it, and only what is in scope
- **What you saw.** The Digest's loop started on its own 30 seconds after the server came up and again every six hours, and for every tenant profile with a remembered secret it read FP/TP Detection Ratio, the SaaS tenants and the packs' drift whatever the profile's Scope page said. Every one of those reads counts against the tenant's ZIA API rate limit.
- **Now: off unless you opt in.** The server's timer does not start unless `DLP_CHECK_CONTINUOUS_EVAL_HOURS` is set to a number of hours above 0 (unset or 0, the default: off; a value that is not a number, below 1 hour, above 8784 hours or not finite is logged once at startup and treated as off). Only one run reads the tenants at a time: a second *Run the loop now* while one is running gets 409, and the timer skips its pass. A run in which any tenant read failed (a 429, say) shows status *partial*, and `--once` exits 1. A profile with nothing in scope is counted as *not read*, not as evaluated. *Run the loop now* on the Digest tab, `python continuous_eval.py --once` and `RUN_Continuous_Evaluation.bat` still run it when you ask. The Digest tab shows *Timer: off* with the setting to change; `local_settings.bat.example`, `local_settings.env.example`, `docker-compose.yml`, README, GETTING_STARTED, SECURITY and the overview say the same.
- **Only what is in scope.** Each profile is read for what its saved Scope page puts in scope: FP/TP Detection Ratio and pack drift only when DLP is in its scopes; the SaaS tenants only when SaaS Security (CASB) is marked as run on Zscaler; drift only for the packs ticked on the profile (never every pack) and only on the DLP channels marked as run on Zscaler (Inline Web, Endpoint, SaaS Security, Email). A channel covered by an alternative, a gap or no choice is not read. A profile with no scopes saved is not read at all (no client is built) and the digest says *nothing in scope*; each skipped section is listed as *Not read (not in scope)*.
- **Round 2: what the loop reads, and what it reports.** FP/TP Detection Ratio now reads the rules of the DLP channels marked as run on Zscaler only (it read every channel's rules and the SaaS application tenants before), checks the Web DLP incident forwarding only when Inline Web is in scope, and reads no Workflow Automation incidents when another product covers Workflow Automation. With DLP in scope but no DLP channel on Zscaler (the Scope page's default), the tenant is not read. A pack's drift is measured on the channels it ships rules for that are in scope; the loop no longer adds a monitor rule the pack never shipped, which made a pack deployed as packed look as if it had moved down. A read that failed (a 429, say) inside FP/TP Detection Ratio or drift is now an error of the run: status *partial*, the profile's status *error*, `--once` exits 1, and no proposal or journey history comes from it. One run at a time now holds across processes: `continuous_eval.py --once` and `RUN_Continuous_Evaluation.bat` take the same lock (`digests\.loop.lock`, released by the OS if the process dies) as *Run the loop now* and the timer; whichever starts second reads nothing (409 in the app, exit code 2 on the command line). Two runs in the same second keep both digests. The audit line counts the profiles read (`tenants`) apart from those not in scope (`tenants_not_in_scope`), with every error of the run. A profile without DLP in scope no longer reports its open proposals as new on every run. On the Digest tab, a run that finished with errors says so in the warning style, and a 409 says a run is already in progress (nothing failed) and shows that run's digest when it lands.
- **Round 3: drift reads only the in-scope part too.** Measuring a ticked pack's drift read the tenant's Web DLP rules whatever the Inline Web choice on the Scope page, and for a pack with Email rules also the SaaS application tenants while SaaS Security was not on Zscaler, though the digest said they were not read. Drift now reads the Web DLP rules only when Inline Web is marked as run on Zscaler, and the SaaS application tenants only when SaaS Security is. With Inline Web out of scope, the tenant counts as unreadable when both of the reads that remain (dictionaries and engines) fail.

### Beacon's tenant API watch reads only what is in scope
- **What you asked for.** "Limit the GETs only to what is selected in the scope assessment": every tenant read counts against the customer's ZIA API rate limit, and the watch read all ~86 endpoints the app's checks use whatever the Scope page said.
- **Now.** The watch reads only the endpoints that belong to the scopes ticked on the Scope page and the channels marked **Zscaler** there. For every scope, alternative and unticked channels are out of scope for the watch, exactly as for the assessment: a check is read for only when the assessment would run it, and a channel's own configuration (Web, Endpoint, Email and SaaS DLP rules, SSL inspection rules, Cloud App Control rules, tenancy restrictions, ...) only with that channel on Zscaler. Which endpoint belongs to which scope is worked out from the checks themselves (`beacon_scope.py`): a scope's check registry, every endpoint path each check and its helpers name (whichever helper it is handed to, imported helpers included), and the channel each check belongs to; a test runs every check against a recording tenant to keep the map honest. An endpoint no check reads is listed as unmapped and never read (today there is none); nothing falls back to reading everything.
- **Where the scope comes from.** The Beacon tab sends the Scope page's selection (live from the assessment tab that opened it, else the ticks the Scope page publishes in this browser on every change, never the last *Save config* copy), with each channel's choice only: the channel notes stay in the browser. With nothing sent it uses the chosen tenant profile's saved scopes and channels; with nothing there either, the run is refused: *Nothing is in scope — choose what to assess on the Scope page.* That is checked before signing in, so the tenant is not touched; so is a sign-in that can read nothing in scope (the classic ZIA API sign-in with only non-ZIA scopes, ZPA without a customer id).
- **On the page.** Before you run, the tenant watch says what it will read (for example *12 endpoints for DLP (Web, Email), ZCC*); the running banner and the results say the same, with the endpoints that were not in scope listed and never read. When the scope differs from the last watch of the same tenant, only the endpoints read both times are compared and the change of scope is said. A watch that read nothing (the tenant did not answer) is said as such, ends the run in error, and is not what the next watch compares with. The pacing is about one HTTP GET a second, including the extra GETs one endpoint can take (Cloud App Control's per-type lists), and the request count is the GETs actually sent. `POST /api/beacon/scope` answers the same question without contacting a tenant.
- **Second review.** The endpoints a check reads through a helper the GET-call pattern does not see (`_items(cl, "/zia/firewallIpsRules")`, ZPA's server groups, connectors and service edges, `casbTenant/lite`, ...) are now watched with their scope, so every endpoint an in-scope check reads is either read or listed as not read. The same channels sent in another order are the same scope, and a channel that brings nothing in (Web DLP marked Zscaler with only ZCC ticked) is neither named in the summary nor a scope change. A watch that stopped on its 900-second budget says how many endpoints in scope were not read, ends the run as *partial*, and is not the baseline while a complete watch is kept; a watch where no GET got an HTTP answer (the tenant could not be reached) reads as a watch that read nothing. The GET count includes GETs that timed out or were retried. The preview sends the same sign-in as the run (typed on the assessment tab, a profile, or saved on this computer), so it refuses exactly what the run refuses, and every scope unticked on the Scope page is *Nothing is in scope*, never the profile's saved selection. Working out the map takes about a second instead of half a minute, once, however many previews ask at the same time; a preview still being answered is not asked again on a window focus. On the page, polling no longer closes the *not in scope* list or moves keyboard focus, a failed watch reads as failed, and its change is labelled *read nothing*.

### The Terraform hand-off says which hand-off to use, and when
- **What you saw.** Gap mode and static were described by what they do, not by when to pick them, and gap mode was picked for you whenever tenant details were filled in.
- **Now.** Step 2 is *Which hand-off*: a table from the customer's situation to the choice: Deploy policy packs when they change Zscaler in the portal; gap mode when they manage it as code and the tenant already has DLP objects; static for an empty tenant, no credentials, a reference to review or an uploaded pack; neither until object ownership is agreed when they also edit in the portal. Each mode lists *Use when* and *Not when*. Static is the default; gap mode, which reads the tenant (and counts against the ZIA API rate limit), runs only when you pick it.

### A run paused for ZIA's rate limit counts down to when it sends again
- **What you saw.** "The timer isn't resuming when the threshold is met": after "Resource Access Blocked" the removal window said "Working…" for the whole 54-minute pause while the clock kept climbing, with no sign of when it would go on. The run was not stuck (it resumed on schedule, as it had after an earlier pause in the same run), but nothing on screen said so.
- **Now.** While a deploy or removal is paused for ZIA's rate limit the window says *Paused for ZIA's rate limit — resumes at 1:00 PM (in 13:05). Nothing is sent meanwhile*, counting down every second, then *ZIA's pause is over — sending again…*. The job status (`/api/dlp-packs/jobs/{id}`) carries `paused_seconds` and `paused_until` for that tenant's pause only. The clock on the right is still the whole run's time.

### Your six Secure by Design decisions: SSO per person (still off by default), who did what, writer groups, job logging, a warning banner
- **What you decided.** Asked one at a time; you picked the recommended option each time, and confirmed that SSO stays off by default and is only a setting you turn on.
- **SSO is off by default.** Nothing changes for a normal install or the container on its default loopback publish: the UI token works as before and SSO is never offered. Turn it on with `ZSCALER_SSO_ENABLED=1` plus the `ZSCALER_SSO_*` settings (SECURITY.md).

**When SSO is on**
